# The L3 application dispatch — the points its release must deal with, gathered (2026-11-09)

**What this file is.** A working list for the Cowork session that writes the release surface of
`records/cc/instructions/cc_instruction_l3_application_2026_11_08.md` (the dispatch; 593,140 bytes at the instructions
folder's listing when this list was gathered). It gathers, by where they sit in the dispatch, the points entry 604 §3
step 4 and entry 665 §3 step 2 send to the release: the writing side's authored settlements the user may overrule
before Task 0, and the points the ledger (§L) leaves *"for the releasing sitting"*. **It decides nothing, puts nothing
to the user and is not a surface.** Documentation only; no algorithm change. The user's word for gathering first,
sorting next: "yes" (2026-11-09, the sitting booted on entry 665).

**How it was gathered.** A search for *"Open, for the releasing sitting"* meets 51 lines: one each at §L.4 to §L.6,
§L.8 and §L.10 to §L.55, and one inside §L.3's §9 note (line 451). Each was read at its line with the check-turn note
inside it, except §L.20's, §L.21's and §L.22's (lines 1731, 1813, 1881) and §L.55's (line 4890), which were taken from
the check row (line 20): it records the first three as having nothing left for the releasing sitting and §L.55 as
leaving nothing new. A point a check note marks *settled* or *answered* is left
out; a point it marks *stands* is listed. Also read: the construction-state table (lines 12–20); the forms section at
lines 145–186 (the end of form (a) through form (e)); Task 1 (c) (lines 5029–5068); Task 3 (c) (lines 5172–5223); Task 4's *Not written* list (lines 5404–5426); a
search for *"overrul"* and for *"[AT RELEASE]"*. **Not read for this list:** the ruling records, the surfaces, the
carry list, the reading file. Line numbers are the dispatch's as it stood at 593,140 bytes.

**How it is grouped.** By the question, so that a question repeated across entries appears once with the sites this
gathering found beside it. The grouping is the writing side's; a point may belong under two groups, and the sorting session may
regroup. Row numbers are given as the entries give them.

carried*; these records write a bare *"ADOPTED"*. Sites: §L.5 (1) (Rows 2.7 (iii), 7.108); §L.8 (1) (Row 7.95, the
record's *"carried"*); §L.13 (1) (seven rows); §L.25 (1) (25 row-parts); §L.26 (1) (11); §L.27 (1) (14; Rows 38.47 and
38.48 also need part E's narrower wording, whether *as proposed* can be written at all); §L.28 (1) (four; Row 36.106 (i)

at §6 and §5 before it goes to the user.

## Part A, sorted (begun 2026-11-09 by the sitting booted on entry 666)

Documentation only; no algorithm change. Each group below is either stated as a derivation (for the user to ratify on
the release surface, D-599) or kept as a question. Groups not listed here are not yet sorted.

**A1 and A2 — derivation, not a question.** Read at the objects: the reading file's §5 (lines 379–396) and the
dispatch's form (b) (lines 155–160); the §6 rows carry their proposed word in full in a paragraph opening
`*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.*` (seen at lines 1806, 1979 and 2110 of the reading file; the rows of each site were not read
one by one). §5 defines *ADOPTED — carried* as content a **derived** statement already carries, *"named by its
L3-S-number"*, and *ADOPTED — proposed* as content no derived statement carries, a proposal that it be added. Form (b)
already says a row placed *as proposed* gets its line *saying as proposed*. From these, without a new choice:
(i) where a record writes a bare *"ADOPTED"* and names no L3-S number, the line's word is the row's own proposed word,
read at its `*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.*` paragraph; if that word is *ADOPTED — proposed*, the line says **ADOPTED —
proposed**, *as proposed*; (ii) where the record names an L3-S number as carrying the content, the word is **ADOPTED —
carried**, with that number; (iii) where the record says *"carried by"* an added statement (§L.42, §L.51), the word is
**ADOPTED — proposed**, because an added statement has no L3-S number and is not a derived statement, and the line's
placement quotes the record's *"carried by …"* words; (iv) where the ruling adopts narrower content than the proposal
(§L.27 part E, Rows 38.47, 38.48; §L.28 Row 36.106 (i)), the line does not say *as proposed*; it gives the placement in
the record's words; (v) where the row's proposed word is anything but an ADOPTED word, or the row carries no
`*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.*` paragraph (§L.40's rows are to be checked for this), that row is a STOP-and-report. This rule
is applied row by row by the act; this sitting did not apply it to any row. **To do at release:** (Ruled: Ruling 9 of the part 2 ruling record, `records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_11_l3_application_release_part2_sitting.md` (its heading at that record's line 363, its *To do at release* at lines 417–419), governs this point; the proposal after this note is kept as former wording (#12). Pointer added at release, step 5 part 4, 2026-11-17, by the sitting booted on entry 1077, on the release check's finding 42; nothing replaced.) write (i) to (v) into
form (b), and change each A1 and A2 *Open* point to point to it, former wordings kept.

**§L.6 (1)** (2026-11-10, the sitting booted on entry 668). Read at the objects: the cadence-type record whole (its §1b,
Ruling 3 items b to d); the cadence-features record whole (its §2 item c); the dispatch's §L.6 (lines 583–674) and §L.11

(v) **Row 7.104**, proposed **UNPLACED** (outgoing: *"every admitted authentic motion is perfect or imperfect, with no
third outcome"*): **HISTORICAL**, quoting item b (*"the perfect/imperfect split is computable wherever its features carry
values; type not computable only where a feature is missing"*). Item b admits the third outcome the row excludes, so
the row's rule is a superseded plan (§5). The record does not write *"decided against"*, so form (b-H)'s search would
not find the row; the line is written from this derivation.
(vi) **Rows 8.37 (i) and 8.38 (i)**, each proposed **ADOPTED — proposed** at §6 (8.37 (i)'s proposal: *"that L3 never
attempt to identify a melody or structural line among the voices"*; 8.38 (i) *"travelling with Row 8.37 (i)"*):
**ADOPTED — proposed**, by A1 and A2 rule (i), the line not saying *as proposed* but giving the placement in the
records' words, by rule (iv): item d's *"follow from Ruling 1c of the 2026-11-03 cadence-features record"* and that
item's rule, *"L3 never attempts to identify a structural melody among the voices"*. The ruled words (*"a structural
melody"*) differ from the proposal's (*"a melody or structural line"*); whether they mean the same is not judged here,
and quoting the records' words is right either way.

  57.451 at §3.1 *"ADOPTED — proposed, as rules about the catalog L3 reads"* and ratifies the placements *"as the
  surface states them"*. Each other word fails at its own definition: not ADOPTED — carried, for §6 names no derived
  statement; not RELOCATED, for the record reads it as a rule about the catalog L3 reads; not QUARANTINED, for §6 reads
  the sentence as a rule and not as the code as built; not DISCARDED, for no worth-test finding is given; not
  HISTORICAL, for the record says the part follows. Whether the line says *as proposed* is A2's question for §L.38
  (1)'s five rows, and this part takes what they take. **The other part: HISTORICAL**, as the §L.38 table already
  writes. Both lines come from the one record, so form (b)'s two-record rule is not met, and point (2)'s
  STOP-and-report does not fall on the row.
- **Row 57.396 (ii)**, proposed **UNPLACED** at §6 (the catalog *"never defines or re-implements"* the licensed root
  motion): nothing waits. **The part that follows: ADOPTED — proposed**, a line for that part, giving the placement in
  the record's words (*"the catalog defines none of L2's chord-pair knowledge"*). Grounds: the record names no word and
  the surface gives no *"as"* link, so the word is derived here. The surface's §3.4 rests the part on key Ruling 1
  (*"reference L3 reads; the table is L2's"*), the ground its §3.1 gives Row 57.493 (i); and the part's content is
  §3.1's two stores neither derived from the other (Rows 57.398 (i), 57.495 (i)), stated from the catalog's side
  **[this sitting's reading]**, to which Ruling 1 gives ADOPTED — proposed in terms. Each other word fails as for Row
  57.494 (i); not QUARANTINED, for the part says what the catalog does not define, not how the code works. **The other
  part: HISTORICAL** (*"the "licensed root motion" it names"*), as the table writes. One record, so form (b)'s
  two-record rule is not met. Part (i) of the row is not this record's and is not touched.
- **Row 24.6**, one claim at §6, proposed **UNPLACED** (*"The choice between one store and two by declared design is not
  to be settled by drift: it is made, explicitly, when the component that queries this catalog is built."*): nothing
  waits. **The timing half: HISTORICAL**, as the table writes. **The rest (*"is not to be settled by drift"*, which the
  surface calls met by the rulings): a question; the records do not settle it.** The record names no word and the
  surface gives no link. Weighed: ADOPTED — proposed would propose adding to the derived specification a rule on how a
  choice is to be made that the rulings have already made (the surface's §1.3: *"made by the rulings, explicitly"*);
  HISTORICAL would read the requirement, now met, as a status, though §5 gives HISTORICAL to what is *"not a rule"* and
  the sentence is worded as one; the other words fail (§6 names no derived statement; the sentence is about the
  catalog L3 reads; it does not describe the code; no worth-test finding is given). **The question for the release
  surface:** does the rest of Row 24.6 take ADOPTED — proposed or HISTORICAL? Either only files documentation. Until
  the user rules, point (2)'s STOP-and-report stands for the row.
- **Rows 24.4 and 38.79 (ii)**, each proposed **UNPLACED** at §6: a part of each waits on DP-O's catalog term (key

(xxxii) **Ruling 1's seven items** (38.81 (ii); 38.82, its second part; 57.107 (ii), its predictive half; 57.445 (ii);
57.433 (ii), its second half; 46.27, its composing half; 46.37 whole): **a line now at each row, of a form with no
disposition word, giving the placement in the record's words. A derivation, not a question.** At §6 each is proposed
**UNPLACED**: 38.81 (ii) and 38.82 *"travelling with Row 38.21 (ii)"*; 57.445 (ii) *"read as at Row 38.21 (ii)"*;
57.433 (ii) *"read as at Row 38.20"*; 46.27 *"read as at Row 44.4"*; 46.37 *"read as at Row 7.85"*; 57.107 (ii)
**[relayed from (xxviii)]**. For 38.82, 57.107 (ii), 57.433 (ii) and 46.27 the proposal covers the whole row or part, of
which Ruling 1 places one half. Grounds:
- The reading file's §5 defines UNPLACED as *"NOT a sixth disposition — it is a row the user places"* **[as (x) and the
  surface's §1 quote it]**. Ruling 1 is that placement: *"placed by the user outside every charter: the design of a
  future chord-suggestion tool, carried for that tool's own specification"*.
- The record's §4 says the rulings *"re-mark no row (the application act does, route record §2 step 2)"*: the act
  writes at the row what a ruling places.
- Ruling 1 gives no disposition word on purpose: *"Alternatives not taken: A, HISTORICAL … B, ADOPTED — proposed as
  dormant notes"*, the surface's §4 having set out why none of the five words fits. Form (b)'s line needs a word, so it
  cannot be used here without going against the ruling, and no word is derived.
- Writing nothing at the row, the notes list being the items' only place, was weighed and not taken: the row would keep,
  as its only disposition, an UNPLACED that by §5's definition still waits on the user, and the reading file would not
  say where the row went, which alternative C, as ratified, named among its grounds (*"the label says where the rows
  went; the UNPLACED definition ("a row the user places")"*). The notes list sits in the successor, not at the row.
- The dispatch already has a ruled line with no disposition word: form (c), *"No disposition word is written, and the
  row is not counted in the ruled distribution"* (line 171). The line here takes that shape.
So: ONE line added beneath the row's `*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.*` paragraph, of the form `*RULED PLACEMENT (<record file>,
Ruling 1).* Outside every charter — <the record's placement in its words>; listed in the successor's notes.`, naming
the part where Ruling 1 places a part only (form (b)'s split-row rule). No disposition word is written; the row is not
counted in the ruled distribution and is listed in the report as placed outside every charter. Where another record
gives another part of the same row a line, this line carries no word that could differ from it; whether form (b)'s
two-record rule works by row or by part is A8's and is not touched. **The wording of this form is the writing side's**,
as forms (a) to (e) are, and the user may overrule it before Task 0; it goes on the release surface with Part B.
**Not touched here:** the other A5 sites (§L.40 (2), §L.41 (2), §L.42 (4), §L.43 (1)), each resting on its own record;
Row 4.17's three chord-name items at (xvii).
**Documentation only; no algorithm change.** The method stands as the record states it, quoted at (xxviii); Ruling 1
adds that the design of a future chord-suggestion tool lies outside the analysis, and nothing on L3's *Publishes* line,
as the key record quotes it and as key Ruling 1 extends it, suggests chords **[relayed from the record's §2]**. Not
applied to any row by this sitting. §L.39's *Open* points (2) (A2), (3) (sorted at (xxviii)), (4) (A17) and (5) are not
touched here. **To do at release:** write the form into the forms section after form (c); change §L.39's *Open* point
(1) to point here; write the form into the §L.39 table's line for the seven (dispatch line 3359), which now makes each a

precedents"*; the line records the placement and needs no more. The record's §2, *"The nine rows of 2C wait there"*,
concerns Δ7 and the register status of D-033 and D-034, the framework's and the ratification act's; it does not hold
back the line, which says where the user placed the rows.

§L.43 table writes them). The form's wording stays the writing side's and goes on the release surface with Part B, as
(xxxii) says.

key and chord together and publishes the chord as a degree in its tonality, and the tonality as tonic and mode, two

**§L.44 (1)** (2026-11-10, the sitting booted on entry 686). Read at the objects: the presets-scope record whole; its
surface, `ratification_surfaces/cowork_l3_presets_scope_family_surface_2026_11_07.md`, whole; the dispatch's §L.44
(lines 3794–3889), its forms section from form (a)'s last items through form (e) (lines 140–186) and Task 3 (c) (lines
5172–5223); the reading file's §5 (lines 379–401), Rows 23.30, 23.31, 23.36, 23.37, 23.39, 23.40 and 23.41 at §6, read
by offset at the lines the surface gives (lines 32773–32812 and 32868–32963), and §10 at its opening (lines 78925–78939) and from its
heading *"To *the measurement of the analysis*"* (line 79797) to line 79901; the L2 reading file at Row 25.34 (lines
43388–43405), the twin of Row 23.30, with its two ruled lines.
(xxxvii) **2A's seven row-parts** (23.30 (i); 23.31; 23.36 (i); 23.37; 23.39; 23.40; 23.41): **RELOCATED, form (b), the
line carrying the record's own words for the stretch. A derivation, not a question.** §L.44's *Open* point (1) asks
whether §5's RELOCATED admits a home outside the framework, or whether the line must carry the record's *"stretched"*
words. Both halves are answered at the objects:
- **§5 alone does not admit it.** §5 defines RELOCATED as content that *"belongs to another charter"*, the row naming
  *"that charter in `FRAMEWORK.md` §5's own words"* (lines 387–388), and lists those charters (lines 398–401): L0, L1,
  L2, *the second axis — voice leading*, *the uncertainty surface* and *the measurement of the analysis*.
  `ARCHITECTURE.md` §6.8 is none of them. At §6 each of the seven is proposed **UNPLACED** on that ground (Row 23.30
  (i): *"no charter of `FRAMEWORK.md` §5 names the user-facing preset layer"*; the other six in the same terms).
- **The ruling stretches the word in terms and orders the stretch written beside the rows.** Ruling 1, 2A: *"RELOCATED —
  outside the framework, to `ARCHITECTURE.md` §6.8, the user-facing preset layer, recorded and deferred product work
  (7)"*, and *"Recorded beside the rows, as L2 Ruling 1 records: RELOCATED stretched to a home outside the framework;
  the transfer list's entry "outside the framework — the style system"."* The user's word was *"yes"*. The alternatives
  he declined include *"L3's own label "outside every charter, carried for the style system's preset layer""*, which is
  (xxxii)'s form, and *"For any part, left UNPLACED"*. So the line carries the record's words; it is not (xxxii)'s form,
  for this ruling gives a disposition word and the user declined that label for these rows.
- **The model is the L2 twin's ruled line**, read at the object (L2 reading file, Row 25.34, lines 43403 and 43405):
  *"**RELOCATED — outside the framework** — to `ARCHITECTURE.md` §6.8, "the user-facing preset layer, recorded and
  deferred product work", placed from UNPLACED"*, followed by the L2 record's *"Recorded beside the rows"* sentence and
  its sentence that the style system's decisions stay LIVE at their homes. The L3 record's surface proposed the L2
  wording *"so that one sentence carries one label at both layers"* (its §2A), and Ruling 1 ratified it.
So each of the seven gets the line `*RULED DISPOSITION (<record file>, Ruling 1).* **RELOCATED — outside the framework** —
to `ARCHITECTURE.md` §6.8, the user-facing preset layer, recorded and deferred product work; recorded beside the row:
RELOCATED stretched to a home outside the framework; the transfer list's entry "outside the framework — the style
system"; D-587, D-589 and D-590 stay LIVE at their homes and are not written into L3's text.`, each part quoted from the
record's §1 (the L2 line's *"placed from UNPLACED"* is not in the L3 record and is not written). Rows 23.30 and 23.36 name claim (i) only (form (b)'s split-row rule); their
claims (ii) are proposed RELOCATED at §6 (to L2 and to *the uncertainty surface*) and are the record's §2, carried, not
placed (§L.44's *Open* point (3), under A16).
**The transfer list.** Task 3 (c) item 5 makes a destination with no §10 heading a STOP-and-report, no heading made. The
record names *"the transfer list's entry "outside the framework — the style system""* among the words recorded *"as L2
Ruling 1 records"*, and the L2 ruling those words repeat says *"The transfer list (§10 of the reading file) gains one
entry"* (as Row 25.34's ruled line quotes it). Read so, the L3 record orders the same entry in the L3 reading file's §10
**[this sitting's reading; the record does not name the L3 file's §10 in terms]**, and item 5's own opening sentence
says *"where a record's own words and this settlement differ, the record governs and the difference is reported"*.
Whether §10 already carries such a heading was not read: §10 was read only at the lines named above, and no search was
run over the reading file. **To do at release:** read §10's headings by offset; if no heading *"outside the framework —
the style system"* stands, write into Task 3 (c) item 5 that for these seven rows the record's entry is made as the

(xlix) **Form (b)'s two-record rule applies by part: two records' lines on different parts of a row are each written,
whatever their words; only two lines on the same part with differing dispositions make a STOP-and-report. A derivation,
not a question.**
- **What the rule says now.** *"A row named by two records gets both records' lines, in ledger order, and is listed in
  the report under *rows named by more than one record*; where the two lines' dispositions differ, that row is a
  STOP-and-report and gets neither."* (dispatch lines 157–159). Read by row, Rows 38.79 (i), 24.2 and 57.454 would each
  be a STOP-and-report (§L.36's *Open* point (4), lines 3125–3128).
- **The unit of a disposition is the part, by the dispatch's own forms and by §5.** Form (b) itself gives a split row one
  line per claim (*"the line names that claim"*, line 160), and form (b-H) gives a decided-against part its own
  HISTORICAL line while *"the rest of the row keeps what the record says of it"* (lines 163–164). One record may so give
  one row lines with different words: §L.36's own table gives Row 38.22 (ii) ADOPTED — proposed, HISTORICAL and
  RELOCATED lines (line 3073), and this list has others ((xxxiv), Row 57.514, four lines; (xxxiii), Row 48.20 (ii), two).
  The reading file's §5 keeps one disposition per row-part, as (xx) records it **[relayed from (xx); §5 not read by
  this sitting]**. Read by row, the rule would stop a row when
  two records split it, and let it through when one record splits it the same way; nothing in form (b) gives a ground
  for that difference.
- **The ratified records divide these three rows between them by part, in terms.** The earlier record hands a part over,
  and the later record places that part and no other: Row 38.79 (i), lead-rows *"the remainder ("idiom-specific") going
  with the idiom-tag rows"* (§L.30, line 2563); Rows 24.2 and 57.454, recognition part 2 *"the idiom weighting moved to
  the idiom-tag surface"* and *"the idiom filter and weight vector moved to the idiom-tag surface"* (§L.32, line 2737);
  and the idiom-tags part 1 record ratifies *"the 8 pieces moved to this surface by the lead-rows, recognition part 1 and
  2, further-rows and substitution part 2 rulings"* (its §0), these three among them (§L.36, lines 3095–3096). A by-row
  reading would leave unwritten what the user ratified in both records. Form (b) is the writing side's wording (as
  (xxxii) says of forms (a) to (e)), and is not to be read so as to leave unwritten what two rulings place.
- **The three rows, by part.** At §6 each is one claim, proposed **UNPLACED** (lines 33255, 47952, 76896); the parts
  are the records' words, as form (b-H) names a part.
  - **Row 38.79 (i)**: **HISTORICAL** from the lead-rows record, for *"in so far as a preset selects the catalog L3
    reads"*; from the idiom-tags record, **HISTORICAL** for *"a catalog per idiom decided against"* and **ADOPTED —
    proposed** for *"entries specific to idioms ADOPTED — proposed as their tags"*. The ADOPTED line falls on the
    remainder the lead-rows record handed over and gave no line; the two HISTORICAL lines carry the same word.
  - **Row 24.2**: from recognition part 2, **RELOCATED** for *"the prior"* and **ADOPTED — proposed** for *"the
    annotation"*; from the idiom-tags record, **RELOCATED** for *"the weighting as evidence"* and **HISTORICAL** for *"a
    weight deciding which matches L3 publishes"*. The HISTORICAL line falls on the idiom weighting, which part 2 moved and
    gave no line.
  - **Row 57.454**: from recognition part 2, **ADOPTED — proposed** for *"the decided chords and the catalog as
    inputs"*; from the idiom-tags record, **HISTORICAL** for *"the filter … in so far as it selects the catalog L3
    reads"* and **RELOCATED** for *"the weight vector"*. Both fall on what part 2 moved and gave no line. The outgoing
    sentence names the catalog as *"queried, filtered per §4.5"*: the querying is part 2's input, the filtering the
    idiom-tags record's.
  So no part of any of the three carries two lines with differing words. Each row is listed in the report under *rows
  named by more than one record*, as form (b) already says.
- **The other items that deferred to A8 do not change.** (xxviii) (Row 57.107 (ii)), (xliv) (Row 38.26) and (xlv) (Row
  40.40) found the same word on the two parts, so they stood either way. (xxxix) left to A8 whether Row 57.118's two
  RELOCATED lines, from two records on different parts, meet the rule: by part, they do not, and both are written, each
  to its own destination.
**The wording, to be written into form (b) at release** (the writing side's, overrulable before Task 0 with Part B): in
place of the sentence quoted above, *"A row named by two records gets each record's lines, in ledger order, and is
listed in the report under *rows named by more than one record*. The rule is applied by part, a part being what a
record's line names: where two records' lines fall on the same part with differing dispositions, that part is a
STOP-and-report and gets neither of those lines; lines on different parts are each written. A part a record says it
moves to another record gets no line from the record that moves it."* Form (d)'s *"follows form (b)'s two-record rule"*
(line 177) then reads by part too, with no change of its words.

**§L.48 (1)** (2026-11-10, the sitting booted on entry 696). Read at the objects: the chord-span pair record whole; its
surface, `ratification_surfaces/cowork_l3_chord_span_pair_group_surface_2026_11_08.md`, whole; the disposition-words
record whole and its surface, `ratification_surfaces/cowork_l3_disposition_word_surface_2026_11_08.md`, whole; the
application-surface record whole; the dispatch's form (b) and (b-H) (lines 155–167), §L.2's line for the two rows (lines
335–336), §L.48 (lines 4161–4257) and §L.55 at lines 4860–4888, and a search of the dispatch for *"57.511"*; the reading
file's §5 (lines 379–401) and, at §6, Rows 19.39 and 57.511 read by offset from their headings (lines 24490, 75212).
(l) **Rows 19.39 (ii) and 57.511: HISTORICAL, form (b-H), from the chord-span pair record's Ruling 1; Row 19.39's line
names claim (ii) only. The disposition-words record's *"stay UNPLACED"* gives no line, so no part carries two lines. A
derivation, not a question.**
- **The order of the sittings.** The chord-span pair sitting booted on entry 589; the application-surface sitting on
  entry 601; the disposition-words sitting on entry 606 (each record's head). So the *"stay UNPLACED"* is the later
  ruling, and the HISTORICAL placement the earlier. This list's A9 heading said the reverse (corrected there, former
  wording kept). §L.48's *Open* point (1) states the order correctly (dispatch lines 4225–4227).
- **What the later ruling rests on.** Its surface's §6 gives the ground, whole: *"Neither record places them. Whether
  L3-S8 decides against the nesting depends on whether L1's change points can fall inside one of L2's chord spans. This
  sitting did not check that. **They stay UNPLACED, the honest default**"*. *"Neither record"* means the two early
  phrase records its §0 names (the phrase-ends and phrase-units records). Its §8 lists the same fact under *"Not
  checked"*. The surface does not name the chord-span pair record, and its record (Ruling 1, §6) adds no ground of its
  own.
- **What UNPLACED is.** The reading file's §5 (lines 395–396): *"this session cannot defend one disposition in one
  sentence at the two texts … This is the honest default and it is NOT a sixth disposition — it is a row the user
  places."* So the later ruling names no disposition for the rows; it leaves them for the user to place, on the ground
  that the two early records do not place them.
- **The user had already placed them, on exactly that fact.** The chord-span pair record's Ruling 1 (the user's word
  *"yes"*): *"Rows 19.39 (ii) and 57.511 — Recorded as decided against by phrase-ends Ruling 1 of 2026-11-02 (L3-S8 as
  derived: "Every change point and both span edges are candidate phrase ends"; "no item of evidence forces or forbids a

  span."* That is the fact the later surface left unchecked, answered by a ruling.

  an L3 ruling record says it decides against is re-marked **HISTORICAL** by the application act, its line quoting the
  record's own placement in the record's words."* Form (b-H) writes it so. The chord-span pair surface had named
  HISTORICAL as an alternative not proposed, because the label *"hides that a ruled decision contradicts the
  sentence"*; Ruling 1 of the later application-surface sitting settled the label, and form (b-H)'s line quotes the
  decided-against sentence, so the contradiction is stated in the line.

  *"as Row 19.39 (ii)"*, lines 75236–75237), and neither entry carries a ruled or recorded line.

**§L.12 (1)** (2026-11-10, the sitting booted on entry 699). Read at the objects: the sections record whole (its §2,
Ruling 1, *"Documentation"*); the disposition-words record whole; the dispatch's §L.12 (lines 901–1000), and a search of

the record's placement; for Rows 19.38 (vii), 19.43 (i) and 38.77 (i) the line says *as proposed*, for the other nine it
does not. A derivation, not a question.**
- **What the record says.** Its §2, *"Documentation"*: *"recorded as overtaken by the charter — the twelve rows that put
  sections outside the layer (8.35(ii), 8.36(i), 8.113(i), 8.114(i), 8.115, 8.140, 8.141, 19.38(vii), 19.43(i),
  19.59(iv), 38.77(i), 57.320(i))"*, and, closing the paragraph: *"The comparison file proposes 19.38(vii), 19.43(i) and
  38.77(i) HISTORICAL; this ruling does not change a proposed disposition."* Ruling 1 item (1): *"sections are decided at
  L3, as the charter says"*.
- **What §6 proposes, read at each row.** Rows 19.38 (vii) and 38.77 (i): **HISTORICAL** — *"a status at the time of
  writing"* (38.77 (i) *"as Row 19.38 (vii)"*); Row 19.43 (i): **HISTORICAL**, *"travelling with Row 19.38 (vii)"*. The
  other nine are each proposed **UNPLACED**: Rows 8.35 (ii), 8.36 (i), 8.113 (i), 8.114 (i), 8.115, 8.140, 8.141 and
  57.320 (i) as *"a rule on L3's own subject that a derived statement differs from"* (57.320 (i) *"travelling with Row
  8.36 (i)"*), and Row 19.59 (iv) on *"the two texts above"*. Each of the twelve is DIFFERS against L3-S22.
- **The three: HISTORICAL, as proposed.** The record names their proposal and says it is not changed; form (b) gives a
  row placed as proposed a line *saying as proposed*.
- **The nine: HISTORICAL, not as proposed**, the line giving the placement in the record's words. Grounds: §5 defines
  HISTORICAL as including *"a superseded plan, or a status"*, and the record says the charter overtakes these rows, each
  of which keeps sections out of the layer (*"Default out."*; *"deferred, not forbidden"*; out of the *"core"*; *"a
  larger, *higher*-layer structure"*; the core's four outputs without sections). Each other word fails at its own
  definition: not ADOPTED, for Ruling 1 keeps the opposite; not RELOCATED, for each row's §6 entry calls it a rule on
  L3's own subject; not QUARANTINED, for none states how the implementation works; not DISCARDED, for no worth-test
  finding is given. This is (x)'s pattern for the four section-side rows the same paragraph of the record names, and
  (xi)'s for Row 7.34. The record writes *"overtaken"*, not *"decided against"*, so form (b-H)'s search would not find
  these rows, and the line is written from this derivation.
- **Why the dispatch's present reading does not stand.** §L.12's *Open* point (1) reads *"this ruling does not change a
  proposed disposition"* as covering all twelve, so that *"every one of the twelve keeps its proposal and its line says
  *as proposed*"* (dispatch lines 965–967). For the nine that line would write **UNPLACED** as a ruled disposition, which
  §5 says *"is NOT a sixth disposition — it is a row the user places"*, for rows the user has placed (as at (li)). Read

  sentence on the three; and if it is read as covering all twelve, the nine carry no proposed disposition for it to
  keep, UNPLACED not being one. No weak point is stated for this site.
- **Two records.** The search of the dispatch meets the twelve row numbers at §L.12's line for them, and otherwise only

  form (b)'s two-record rule is not met, by (xlix)'s reading or by the present wording.

- **What §6 proposes.** Each is proposed **ADOPTED — carried** (L3-S24), each AGREES with L3-S24's *"L3 publishes the
  grouping (boundaries, nesting and rivals) and not form-function names"*: Row 8.23 whole (the glossary entry
  *"Caplinian formal functions [MT — out of L6's scope]"*, the sentence and the period); Row 8.35 (i) (the layer does not
  do Caplin's formal functions); Row 8.113 (ii) (periods and sentences are out of the grouping's core).
- **What the record says.** Ruling 3 decides that L3 works out and publishes form-function names, and *"L3-S24 to be
  rewritten accordingly"*. Of these three: they *"concern Caplin's theme types (sentence, period), not the sonata
  labels"*; *"their proposed disposition, "ADOPTED — carried (L3-S24)", no longer stands as proposed, and they are **held,
  travelling with the naming-scheme question**"*. Its *"Not decided by this ruling"* includes *"which naming scheme
  (sonata-form labels, Caplin's theme types, or both)"*; its check correction (1) says their placement *"travels with the
  naming-scheme question rather than being decided against"*; its §4 carries them forward with that question.
- **No disposition word follows.** Not HISTORICAL: correction (1) declines *"decided against"* in terms, and whether
  Caplin's theme types are named at all is open. Not ADOPTED — carried: the record says that proposal no longer stands,
  and L3-S24's text it rests on is to be rewritten. Not ADOPTED — proposed either, which would decide the open naming
  scheme. RELOCATED, QUARANTINED and DISCARDED are proposed by neither §6 nor the record. And a ruled **UNPLACED** would
  write as a disposition a word §5 says is not one, as at (li). So form (b) cannot be used.
- **No line at all does not fit here, unlike (li).** (li) wrote no line because each held row's proposal, UNPLACED,
  stayed true by §5's definition. Here each proposal is an ADOPTED word the record says *"no longer stands as proposed"*;
  with no line, the reading file would show it standing, against the record's words, and would not say where the rows
  went ((xxxii)'s ground). The record names no wording for the reading file.
- **So the line takes (xxxii)'s kind: no disposition word, the record's placement in its words.** ONE line added beneath
  each row's `*PROPOSED DISPOSITION.*` paragraph (for Rows 8.35 and 8.113 naming claim (i) and claim (ii) respectively,
  form (b)'s split-row rule), of the form `*RULED PLACEMENT (cowork_rulings_2026_11_03_l3_sections_sitting.md, Ruling
  3).* Held, travelling with the naming-scheme question — "their proposed disposition, "ADOPTED — carried (L3-S24)", no
  longer stands as proposed".` No disposition word is written; the row is not counted in the ruled distribution and is

  each row keeps its proposed ADOPTED — carried (L3-S24) with nothing at the row saying it no longer stands, and the
  report lists the three.

(lv) **The twelve rows of the step-one remainder record's Ruling 2 that join the phrase-ends record's list of rows
overtaken: HISTORICAL, form (b), each line quoting this record's placement, not *as proposed*. The word follows from
this record's own placement, so no line takes its word from another record; the disposition-words record's lines carry
the same word on the same parts, so both are written. A derivation, not a question.**
- **Which rows.** 8.14 (ii), 8.16 (i), 8.16 (ii), 8.28 (ii), 8.43, 8.60 (i), 8.66, 8.87, 19.41, 38.71, 3.1 (ii), 10.9
  (the record's §2; the §L.52 table, dispatch line 4567). They are not the twelve of (liii), which are the sections
  record's; this list's A11 line in Part A said they were the same, and is corrected there, former wording kept.
- **What §6 proposes, read at each row.** Each part is proposed **UNPLACED** and is **DIFFERS** against L3-S8. Eleven
  give the ground *"a rule on L3's own subject that a derived statement differs from"* (Row 8.14 (ii): *"that one derived
  statement differs from and another agrees with"*; Row 8.16 (ii): *"the same"*; Row 10.9 adds Row 8.14 (ii); Row 38.71
  *"as at Row 19.41"*). Row 3.1 (ii) gives *"read: the two texts above, and the L2 comparison's record that the L0/L1
  comparison relocated the graded phrase-boundary profile to L3"*. Each places the phrase ends outside L3: delimited by
  the phrase-boundary primitive or the notated punctuation (3.1 (ii), 8.14 (ii), 8.28 (ii), 8.60 (i), 10.9, 19.41,
  38.71); the primitive's picked peaks chosen upstream or consumed as they are (8.16 (i) and (ii), 8.43, 8.66); the
  boundary decided upstream and not L6's to override (8.87).
- **What the record says.** Ruling 2 (the user's word *"yes"*): the rows are *"recorded as reached by phrase-ends Ruling
  1"*, *"joining its list of rows overtaken"*; *"Their re-marking is the application dispatch's act"* (its lines 44–55).
  The list joined is the phrase-ends record's: *"The rows of the L3-S8 family that only place the phrase ends in an
  earlier layer or say the grouping layer detects nothing (among them …) are recorded as overtaken by the charter and
  Ruling 48"* (its lines 82–85). The record names no disposition word.
- **The word follows at this record.** §5 defines HISTORICAL as including *"a superseded plan, or a status"* **[as
  (liii) quotes it]**; a plan the record says is overtaken is a superseded plan. Each other word fails at its own
  definition: not ADOPTED, for L3-S8 keeps the opposite (*"The phrase reading is an L3 decision"*); not RELOCATED, for
  the §6 proposals of eleven of the twelve parts call the part a rule on L3's own subject and Row 3.1 (ii)'s cites the record that relocated
  the phrase-boundary profile to L3; not QUARANTINED, for §6 proposes none so and none states how the implementation
  works (Row 8.43 names the primitive's code view, but states what the grouping consumes); not DISCARDED, for no
  worth-test finding is given. This is the derivation (x), (xi) and (liii) make for records that write *"overtaken"*.
  The record does not write *"decided against"*, so form (b-H)'s search would not find these rows, and the line is
  written from this derivation. Each row is proposed UNPLACED, so the line does not say *as proposed*; it gives the
  placement in the record's words.
- **So §L.52's *Open* point (1) does not arise as put.** It asks whether this record's line may take HISTORICAL from the
  disposition-words record (dispatch lines 4618–4621). It need not: the word comes from this record's own placement, as
  above. The disposition-words record's Ruling 1 (the user's word *"yes"*) also gives the same twelve HISTORICAL in
  terms: seven (3.1 (ii), 8.14 (ii), 8.16 (ii), 8.28 (ii), 8.60 (i), 8.87, 19.41) as of the same kind as the phrase-ends
  record's overtaken rows (its surface's §3), and five (8.16 (i), 8.43, 8.66, 10.9, 38.71) as method rows (its surface's
  §4). So the word holds even if the reading of *"overtaken"* above were not ratified. The point's other way, *no line
  from this record*, was weighed and not taken: Ruling 2 places these rows by number and says their re-marking is the
  application act's, so no line would leave unwritten what the user ratified, as (xlix) says where two records each
  place a part.
- **Two records.** Each of the twelve parts gets two lines with the same word: this record's, quoting *"joining its list
  of rows overtaken"* with the phrase-ends record's words, and the disposition-words record's, quoting its own
  placement. For the five, the two placements differ (overtaken here; method rows there), as the §L.52 entry already
  notes (dispatch lines 4599–4601); the word does not. Form (b)'s rule stops only differing dispositions, by its present
  wording and by (xlix)'s, so both lines are written and each row is listed under *rows named by more than one record*.
- **The disposition-words record's check at §6.** That record says *"the act reads each row at §6 and stops and reports
  any that does not fit"* (its lines 37–38). Each of the twelve, read at §6 by this sitting, places the phrase ends
  outside L3, as above, and this sitting found none that does not fit **[this sitting's reading]**. The act's own
  reading is not replaced by this.
- **No weak point is stated for this site** beyond (x)'s reading of *"overtaken"*, which the disposition-words ruling
  makes immaterial to the word here.
**Documentation only; no algorithm change.** The method stands as the record states it (its lines 64–67): L3 decides
phrase ends as a weighed choice over every candidate position, including positions the page does not mark; the
principal partition is published with its rivals and their mass; the old strength profile and the notated markers
enter only as evidence beside the cadential evidence; no marker forces a phrase end and no picked peak decides one.
D-479's partial supersession at L3, ratified with Ruling 2, is the §L.52 *Register* note's and is not touched. Not

the disposition-words record gives these same twelve rows (…). See *Open* point (1)."* with the ground above, pointing
here; in the check note, point *"Points (1) and (2) stand"* (dispatch line 4638) here for (1) and to (xix) for (2);
former wordings kept.

(chord symbol, figured bass as the full figure, harmonic rhythm, cadence with type at each phrase end, phrase and section
grouping, key areas, the pivot re-expression with its flag); L3 publishes no tonic / subdominant / dominant summary and
writes nothing into the score; a display turns published facts into text. Not applied to any row by this sitting. **Not

- **What §6 proposes.** Row 46.43 is one sentence, *"**Style behavior is the consumer's.**"*, proposed **UNPLACED** —
  *"read as at Row 44.1: the consumer is the older stack's recognition consumer, whose recognition no charter of
  `FRAMEWORK.md` §5 names"* (lines 64251–64259). Row 38.22 (ii), the row it travels with, is *"the genre *labels* live in
  the component while genre-aware *behavior* (selection, weights, detection) lives at the consumer"*, proposed
  **UNPLACED** (lines 46973–46986). Row 46.44 is the bullet that 46.43's sentence leads (*"the bullet led *Style behavior
  is the consumer's.*"*, line 64268), and names that behaviour: the preset selecting the active subset, the style
  weighting and the threshold, and style detection.
- **What the ruling says.** Ruling 1 (the user's word *"ratified"*) places *"46.43 — travels with 38.22 (ii)'s behaviour
  half"* (record line 46) and ratifies *"the splits of §3.3"* as written. The surface's row for 46.43, in that §3.3:
  *"travels with 38.22 (ii)'s behaviour half, its three parts placed as there"*, on the ground that recognition part 1's
  surface found it *"the same claim as Row 38.22 (ii)'s second half"* (surface line 181) **[relayed: the idiom-tags
  surface marks this checked at that surface, not read by this sitting]**. The surface places 38.22 (ii)'s behaviour half
  in three parts (its lines 178–180): *selection*, **decided against in so far as it selects the catalog L3 reads**;
  *weights*, **RELOCATED** to ***L2 — The tonal reading. The one entangled decision.***, travelling with key Ruling 2's
  candidate; *detection*, waiting on the open question the L2 tail-style-and-idiom record §1 holds. The dispatch's §L.36
  table gives 38.22 (ii) a HISTORICAL line and a RELOCATED line for those parts, and *"No line for "detection …""* (line
  3073).
- **So a ruling reaches the row by number, and says how it follows.** As at (lvii), the route ruling's *"Rows no family
  reaches stay proposals"* does not reach a row a ratified text names. *"Travels with"* is read as (lvii) found the
  record-placed rows Ruling 1 using it: the travelling row takes its lead's placement. Here the lead is placed by part,
  and the ratified surface says in terms that 46.43's three parts are *"placed as there"*. So the row takes 38.22 (ii)'s
  three behaviour placements, one per part. The parts are named in 38.22 (ii)'s words (*"selection, weights,
  detection"*); 46.43's sentence does not repeat them, and the surface's *"the same claim"* is what carries them over.
- **The lines.** Two form (b) lines, each naming its part (form (b)'s split-row rule): `*RULED DISPOSITION
  (cowork_rulings_2026_11_07_l3_catalog_idiom_tags_part1_sitting.md, Ruling 1).* (selection) **HISTORICAL** — "46.43 —
  travels with 38.22 (ii)'s behaviour half"; as there, selection "decided against in so far as it selects the catalog L3
  reads".` and `*RULED DISPOSITION (… the same record, Ruling 1).* (weights) **RELOCATED** — to ***L2 — The tonal reading.
  The one entangled decision.***: "46.43 — travels with 38.22 (ii)'s behaviour half"; as there, "weights RELOCATED to L2
  with key Ruling 2's candidate".` The row is proposed UNPLACED, so neither line says *as proposed*. The detection part
  gets no line, as 38.22 (ii)'s detection part gets none, and keeps the row's proposed UNPLACED for that part. Form
  (b-H)'s search would not find the selection part: the record's words for 46.43 (line 46) do not say *"decided
  against"*; the hit at line 45 is 38.22 (ii)'s. So the HISTORICAL line is written from this derivation, as at (i), (v)
  and (x).
- **Not A4's question.** A4 asks whether a part that **follows** gets a line before the rest is settled. No part of 46.43
  follows: the labels part of 38.22 (ii), ADOPTED — proposed, is not its behaviour half and does not travel. A
  decided-against part is HISTORICAL by form (b-H) whatever the rest awaits ((xxxi)), and a part the ruling relocates is
  placed by it. The detection part gets what 38.22 (ii)'s detection part gets in the dispatch's table, where no *Open*
  point is raised.
- **Two records.** The search of the dispatch for *"46.43"* meets it at §L.31's table line (2657: *"No line from this
  record."*, the row moved), §L.31's note (line 2680), §L.36 (lines 3074, 3082, 3095, 3108, 3123–3124, 3138–3139) and the
  check row (line 20), which says point (3) still stands. So one record gives the row lines, and form (b)'s two-record
  rule is not met, by its present wording or by (xlix)'s. One record giving one row lines with different words on
  different parts is what §L.36's table already does for 38.22 (ii) itself (line 3073).
- **The transfer list.** The weights part is placed RELOCATED out of UNPLACED, so Task 3 (c) item 5 reaches it: it is
  checked at §10 by row number and appended under the L2 heading if §10 does not already carry it, and not appended again
  if it does (dispatch lines 5204–5209). Whether §10 carries Row 46.43 was not read (no search was run over the reading
  file); item 5 handles both cases, and the L2 heading stands (line 79125, as (xli) read it). Item 6 does not reach the
  row, for §L.36 is not among §L.21 to §L.24.
- **The weak points.** (a) At §6 the row is one sentence. Splitting it into three parts uses 38.22 (ii)'s parenthesis,
  carried over by the recognition part 1 surface's *"the same claim"*, which this sitting did not read. If the user reads
  *"travels with"* as giving the whole sentence one word, no one word exists, for the three parts carry three different
  placements; the row then stays a STOP-and-report, as §L.36's *Open* point (3) has it now. (b) The sentence names no
  selection, weights or detection. The HISTORICAL part decides against it only in so far as its *"style behavior"*
  includes a preset choosing the catalog L3 reads; Row 46.44, the bullet the sentence leads, says it does (*"the preset
  selects the active subset"*). (c) Row 46.44 (iii), style detection *"a future classifier"*, is proposed HISTORICAL at
  §6, and the surface told the user he could place 38.22 (ii)'s detection there; Ruling 1 accepted the surface's
  proposals *"as proposed, none changed"*, so the detection part waits and gets no line here.
**Documentation only; no algorithm change.** The method stands as the record states it (its lines 54–59), quoted at
(xxv): L2 decides key and chord together; DP-O stays open, the catalog term among its unmeasured candidates; L3
publishes the catalog matches once the application act amends `FRAMEWORK.md`; *"The matcher is the same for every
idiom; no preset chooses the catalog; L3's method uses no tag."* Not applied to any row by this sitting. **Not touched
here:** §L.36's *Open* points (1) (A1 and A2), (2) (sorted at (xxv), a question), (4) (sorted at (xlix)) and (5) (settled
by its check note); Row 38.22 (ii)'s own lines. **To do at release:** (Ruled: Ruling 15 of the part 2 ruling record, `records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_11_l3_application_release_part2_sitting.md` (its heading at that record's line 694, its *To do at release* at lines 749–753), governs this point; the proposal after this note is kept as former wording (#12). **§L.3** (2026-11-10, the sitting booted on entry 704). Read at the objects: the phrase-units record whole; the
phrase-ends record at its §0 (lines 1–20) and its Ruling 2 to §4 (lines 94–133); the dispatch's §L.3 (lines 361–453),
§L.18 (lines 1466–1552), §L.19's *Open* note (lines 1620–1644), Task 3 (c) (lines 5172–5223) and Task 5's list of
report sections (lines 5500–5521), and a search of the dispatch for *"§9 point"*, *"reading file's §9"* and
*"OQ-L3-3"*; the reading file's §9 whole (lines 78856–78923).
(lix) **The reading file's §9 points 2 and 8: nothing is written at §9 for them, and they are not listed under *points
answered by a record's own derivation, nothing written*, for a ruling answers them. A derivation, not a question.**
- **What the points are.** §9 quotes the derivation's §7, the eight places where the brief's decomposition *"seemed
  wrong or incomplete"*, each followed by one sentence on the outgoing text, with *"No verdict"* given (lines
  78856–78862). Point 2: *"Faces (b) and (c) share an unnamed object: the alignment of the cadential arrival to the
  phrase end"* (lines 78875–78877). Point 8: *"The charter's sentence on phrase boundaries meets a hard case at elided
  cadences (OQ-L3-3 ★). The faces do not anticipate a phrase end with no notated cue."* (lines 78920–78921).
- **They were put to the user and ruled.** The phrase-units record's §0 names *"the reading file's §9 points 2 and 8"*
  among what was put. Its Ruling 2 (the user's word *"yes"*) ratifies items a to k as derivations, among them item b,
  *"Row 8.84 (i) and §9 point 2 — the cadential arrival (L3-S10) is not held to a fixed window"*, and item j, *"§9 point
  8 answered by Ruling 2 of the morning's record"*. That record is the phrase-ends record of the same day (its sitting
  booted on entry 481, the phrase-units sitting on entry 482), whose Ruling 2 (the user's word *"yes"*) answers OQ-L3-3
  ★: *"A phrase end may fall wherever the evidence as a whole puts it, including at a cadence with no notated cue"*,
  the case point 8 names.
- **Point 5 is listed because its answer is not ruled.** Task 3 (c) item 4 lists it *"for the user"* on the
  harmonic-rhythm record's *"§9 point 5 is answered by L3-S30"*, which stands under that record's heading *"Settled by
  the record — this side's derivation, not a ruling"*; §L.18's amendment 2 calls it *"not ruled; challengeable"*
  (dispatch lines 1471, 1481–1483). So item 4's list brings back to the user an answer he has not ratified. Points 2 and
  8 carry answers he has ratified; listing them under that heading would describe them as unruled.
- **Where the answers are written.** No form writes at §9 (§L.3's note, dispatch lines 450–451; item 4 says the same
  for point 5). Item b is carried whole, its *"§9 point 2"* included, into the successor beside L3-S10 by §L.3's
  amendment 3 (dispatch lines 376–377). Item j's first clause is not among §L.3's amendments (amendment 6 quotes its
  second clause only, lines 382–383); the answer it names is written at OQ-L3-3 in the successor by §L.2's amendment 3,
  *"OQ-L3-3 ANSWERED (Ruling 2)"*, with phrase-ends Ruling 2's bold sentence (dispatch line 297). So the report needs no
  list entry for either point.
- **The weak point.** Item 4's heading is the writing side's wording (under B7). If the user reads *"a record's own
  derivation"* as covering a derivation he has ratified, points 2 and 8 are listed with point 5. Documentation only
  either way.
- **Bears on §L.19 (3)** (§9 point 1, A13's next site): §L.19's note says its answer, §2 (g), *"is not among the items
  Ruling 2 ratifies, so the answer stands as this side's derivation"* (dispatch lines 1631–1632), which is point 5's
  case, not this one. Not sorted here.
**Documentation only; no algorithm change.** The method stands as the records state it: the phrase reading tiles the
working span with phrases and the stretches between them, each unit carrying its kind (phrase-units Ruling 1); the
cadential arrival is not held to a fixed window (item b); a phrase end may fall wherever the evidence as a whole puts
it, including at a cadence with no notated cue, the charter's sentence to be reworded by the application act
(phrase-ends Ruling 2). Not applied to any row by this sitting. **Not touched here:** §L.3's other parts; the other A13

  dispatch's *"The reading file"* is the L3 one, `ratification_surfaces/cowork_comparison_l3_reading.md` (line 78), and

  nothing for them from this record"* (§L.37, lines 3193–3195; in the same terms at §L.42 and §L.45, lines 3681–3682 and
  3937–3939). This record orders nothing for the twins and rules none of them; their RELOCATED to L3 stays a proposal at
  L2, untouched by this act.
- **The second set, the L3 rows: no line; each keeps its proposal, RELOCATED to L2, as a proposal.** At §6 each is
  proposed **RELOCATED** — to ***L2 — The tonal reading. The one entangled decision.***: Row 7.77 (*"which chord
  successions are licensed bears on the chord decision"*, line 4383); Rows 7.78 (i), 7.81, 7.82, 7.83 and 7.84

  motions"* (line 63513); Row 57.396 (i) *"as Row 7.77 placed the licensed root motions and Row 46.1 (ii) the grammar
  that licenses them"* (line 75766). The record places none of them (*"Those rows are not this family's"*). The search
  of the whole dispatch meets Rows 7.77, 7.78, 7.81 to 7.84 and 46.1 (ii) at §L.38 only, and Row 57.396 (i) at §L.38 and
  in §L.33's note naming §L.38 (line 2855); Row 46.1's other claim, (i), is at §L.30, with no line. So no §L entry places
  any of the eight, and the route ruling governs: *"Rows no family reaches stay proposals"*, which the dispatch reads
  *"So a row no record names gets NO line"* (line 101). The act may not choose a word itself (*"No ruling taken"*, *"No
  derivation of any statement"*, lines 5546–5548). Task 3 (c) item 5 reaches RELOCATED placements a §L entry makes, and
  §L.38 makes none (its check note, lines 3318–3319), so nothing is appended at §10 for them.
- **Listed, because the record leaves it open.** By the test (lix) to (lxi) set, a point is listed where its answer is
  unratified or absent. The record states two differences between proposals and ratified placements (the twins'
  RELOCATED to L3 against the HISTORICAL placements of their L3 rows here; the L3 rows' RELOCATED to L2 against L2
  Ruling 1's HISTORICAL for L2's own rows on the yes/no test) and answers neither. As at (lxi), the point is not one
  *answered by a record's own derivation*, for no record derives an answer, nor an *amendment with no words from the
  record*, for none is ordered. It is a point this act may not settle, so it goes under *the points that are not this
  batch's* (Task 5, lines 5518–5521), named there at release so that pointing §L.38's *Open* point (3) here does not
  drop it. Whether the L3 proposals stand against L2 Ruling 1, and whether L2's twins are amended, is left to a later
  ruling; this item decides neither.
- **Two records.** No §L entry gives any of the eight L3 row-parts a line (the search above), so form (b)'s two-record
  rule does not arise. Their other claims are not touched: Row 7.78 (ii) (proposed HISTORICAL at §6), Row 46.1 (i)
  (§L.30, no line) and Row 57.396 (ii) ((xxvii), from this record).

  carries the item as not decided; so it is read here as the writing side's expectation, not an order, as (lvi) read
  the display record's. If the user reads it as an order, the act still has no word for the rows (RELOCATED to L2 as
  proposed, or HISTORICAL following L2 Ruling 1, which the record established for Row 7.77's sentence only), and the
  word would be a question for him. Documentation only either way.

  with it."*, lines 172–173), and its check calls 2B *"a derivation that follows from 2C, not a separate choice"* (§6

  must do if one ever places Row 19.57 (ii) otherwise.

  already says does not place the row *"otherwise"*. The search of the dispatch meets *"19.57"* at §L.43 only (lines
  3742, 3748, 3760 and 3775). So nothing is re-tested, and the HISTORICAL line stands as ruled.
- **Where the condition is kept: at the row, in the record's words.** Form (b)'s line gives *"<placement in the record's
  words>"* (dispatch line 156). The record words 2B's placement in three sentences: the status and what supersedes it;
  where the rule content lives; and when the row is re-tested. Quoting all three puts the condition in the reading
  file, beside the row it would re-test, where a later act that re-places Row 19.57 (ii) will read. That is the record's
  words placed, and the act derives nothing (the standing bars, line 201). The record's §2 says the nine rows of 2C
  *"wait"* at the framework and the ratification act (record lines 67–68), which is where Row 19.57 (ii) could be placed

  listed where its answer is unratified or absent. The condition is ratified, and a line is written, so it is not listed
  under *points answered by a record's own derivation, nothing written*. But the re-test it names belongs to whatever
  later act places Row 19.57 (ii) otherwise, and this act may not change that row's placement. Task 5's bullet *"The

**Documentation only; no algorithm change.** The method stands as the record states it (its lines 45–50): L3 publishes a
cadence only at a phrase end it has decided, reading six features off lower layers in the tonality L2 decided; each
declared vocabulary's type is computed from those features, side by side, none principal; the perfect/imperfect split
is published in two views; a type that cannot be computed carries its reason; a cadence's mass comes only from doubt
already counted, with no fixed, by-rule lowering; L3 reads no voice-leading fact in this specification; L2 reads nothing
L3 publishes. Not applied to any row by this sitting. **Not touched here:** §L.49's *Open* point (2) (answered by its
check note); Rows 7.53, 7.117 and the other row-parts of the line-79174 §10 entry; the other A13 sites. **To do at

**Documentation only; no algorithm change.** The method stands as the bass-role record states it (its lines 53–60): L2
publishes per span the chord (degree, quality, figure, applied target) in its tonality and per sounding note the
chord-tone assignment; L3 publishes the chord symbol whose bass note is the structural bass L2's figure names (L3-S4),
its root spelled with its pitch class (chord-symbol Ruling 1); the span figured bass; the continuo-grain figured bass;
and, per slice, which member of L2's chord the lowest sounding note is, or that it is not a chord tone, named apart from
the structural bass; L3 never re-derives a fact a lower layer publishes (L3-S39). Not applied to any row by this
sitting. **Not touched here:** §L.50's *Open* points (1) and (2) (settled by its check note); §L.17's other *Open*
points; the L3 rows named above; §L.54 (1). **To do at release:** (Ruled: Ruling 31 of the part 2 ruling record, `records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_11_l3_application_release_part2_sitting.md` (its heading at that record's line 1670, its *To do at release* at lines 1736–1740), governs this point; the proposal after this note is kept as former wording (#12). **§L.54 (1)** (2026-11-11, the sitting booted on entry 713). Read at the objects: the application-surface record whole;
its surface, `ratification_surfaces/cowork_l3_application_dispatch_surface_2026_11_08.md`, whole; the route record at its
§2 (lines 48–65); the cadence-features record at lines 74–108, the phrase-ends record at lines 104–115 and the
cadence-type record at lines 62–71; the carry list's §9 and §11 (lines 203–236); the dispatch's head block at lines
40–55, §L.1 at lines 266–273, §L.5 at lines 534–551 and 574–581, §L.6 at lines 612–621, §L.54 (lines 4745–4804), Task
4's *Not written* list and the lines after it (lines 5404–5433) and Task 5's close at lines 5496–5525, and a search of
the dispatch for *"§9 point"*, *"carry list §9"*, *"ratification act"* and *"feature (3)"*; entry 665 §3; this list's
items (lix) to (lxviii).
(lxix) **Carry list §9 points 1 to 4: nothing is added to the dispatch for them; the act writes and lists them as the
dispatch already says; they come back to the user at the ratification act over the new file, whose granularity the
route ruling has that act decide first, in the user's standing form. A derivation, not a question.**
- **What the point is.** §L.54's *Open* point (1) quotes the record's §2a: *"The carry list §9 points 1 to 4 come back to
  the user with or by the application act (the surface §3); whether on one surface or one at a time is written nowhere
  this sitting read."* (dispatch lines 4786–4789); its check note: *"how the points are then put to the user is fixed by
  no Task, and the point stands"* (line 4797). The four, as the carry list's §9 names them (its lines 205–209), each
  read at its record by this sitting: (1) feature (3)'s wording, *"For the application act to bring back to the user."*
  (cadence-features record lines 79–81); (2) the highest-pitch rewording of IC-L1 S-50, *"the rewording is the
  application dispatch's act, brought back to the user with it"* (the same record, lines 104–105); (3) the
  phrase-boundary sentence's exact text, *"its exact text is not ruled by this record and comes to the user with that
  act"* (phrase-ends record lines 110–111); (4) one cadence type per vocabulary, *"this side's reading, not ruled; for
  the application act to bring back to the user"* (cadence-type record lines 67–68).
- **What the act does with them is already written, and agrees with the records.** The head block: *"written by this act
  where the records say and listed in its report for the user. They are not yours to settle."* (dispatch lines 47–49).
  Point 1: the act writes nothing at feature (3) and lists it in its report (§L.5, lines 546–548). Point 2: the act
  writes the record's words at S-50 and lists the item for the user as written (§L.5, lines 539–544; Task 4, lines
  5425–5426). Point 3: a STOP-and-report, listed under *charter and contract amendments left to the user* (Task 4's *Not
  written* item 1, lines 5409–5410). Point 4: nothing written, listed there too (§L.6, lines 614–618; Task 4's item 7,
  line 5423). The records say *"with"* or *"by"* the act, not before it (the carry list's §11, lines 235–236), and three
  of the four concern a wording the act writes or stops on, which can be put only once the act has run (the surface's
  §3). So the release adds nothing to the dispatch for them.
- **Where they come back: the ratification act, by the route ruling.** The route ruling (the user's word *"A"*) has
  three steps: rulings by family; this application act; and *"The ratification act over that new file, its granularity
  decided first."* (route record line 62; the dispatch's §L.1, line 270). Task 5's close names it as the place: *"the
  ratification act is a separate decision surface over the successor (Task 3 (d): §15 of the reading file still reads
  NOTHING), where the points above return"* (dispatch lines 5522–5524), the report's lists, among them *charter and
  contract amendments left to the user*, standing above that line (lines 5510–5513). So *"one surface or one at a time"*
  is a question of that act's granularity, which the route ruling has that act decide first, after this one, not at
  this release. Neither the record's §2a nor the surface's §3 cites the route ruling's step 3 for this point, though
  the surface's sitting read the route record whole (the surface's provenance, its line 167).
- **How they are put then is the user's standing form, not a new choice.** A surface in its own turn, self-contained,
  each point said to be documentation only or an algorithm change; the choice questions in later turns, one decision per
  turn, no widget; where the record already settles a point, say so rather than ask (entry 665 §3 step 3, restating the
  user's rulings of 2026-08-17 and 2026-08-21). So each of the four is put by itself in any case; what is left (whether
  the four share one surface file with the other ratification points) is the granularity the ratification act decides
  first.
- **Not listed again, and not named in Task 5's bullet.** By the test (lix) to (lxviii) set, a point is listed where its
  answer is unratified or absent. The four points themselves are already listed for the user, as above; what §L.54 (1)
  asks is how they are put, and the route ruling answers that by sending it to the ratification act. Task 5's *plan
  lines* bullet already carries the four there, so pointing §L.54's *Open* point (1) here drops nothing.
- **Two records.** The site places no row, so form (b)'s two-record rule does not arise.
- **A fact noted, not acted on.** §L.5 says the act lists point 1 *"in its report"* (line 548) and names no report
  heading. Points 2 to 4 have theirs (*charter and contract amendments left to the user*). Whatever heading point 1 falls
  under, it stands in the report above the *plan lines* bullet, so the derivation above holds for it too.
- **The weak point.** That the route ruling's *"its granularity decided first"* covers how the four points are put, and
  that Task 5's *"the points above return"* takes in all four, are this side's readings. If the user wants the four put
  on a surface of their own before the ratification act's other points, that is a working-practice choice only: it files
  no documentation and changes no algorithm.
**Documentation only; no algorithm change.** Nothing here changes a decided method. The four points stand as their
records leave them: feature (3)'s wording UNSETTLED (the cadence-features record, its first carried bullet); L1
publishes per slice the highest sounding pitch of its sounding set, under that name (cadence-features Ruling 2), S-50's
wording brought back; a phrase end may fall wherever the evidence as a whole puts it, including at a cadence with no
notated cue, the charter sentence's exact text unruled (phrase-ends Ruling 2); every declared vocabulary's types are
published side by side, none principal, the charter's singular read as one type per vocabulary by this side, not ruled
(the cadence-type record). Not applied to any row by this sitting. **Not touched here:** §L.54's other parts; carry list
§9 points 5 to 9 (each at its own record's entry, as the head block's note says, lines 50–54); A14 to A16. **To do at
release:** (Ruled: Ruling 23 of the part 2 ruling record, `records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_11_l3_application_release_part2_sitting.md` (its heading at that record's line 1162, its *To do at release* at lines 1222–1226), governs this point; the proposal after this note is kept as former wording (#12). Pointer added at release, step 5 part 4, 2026-11-17, by the sitting booted on entry 1075, on the release check's finding 42; nothing replaced.) change §L.54's *Open* point (1) (dispatch lines 4786–4789) and its check note's *"how the points are then put
to the user is fixed by no Task, and the point stands"* (line 4797) to point here; former wordings kept.

**§L.29 (1)** (2026-11-11, the sitting booted on entry 714). Read at the objects: the catalog-key record whole; its
surface, `ratification_surfaces/cowork_l3_catalog_key_surface_2026_11_06.md`, at its section headings, its position B
(lines 138–239) and by a search for *"statement"*, *"successor"*, *"amended L3 specification"* and *"detail design"*;
the dispatch's standing bars (lines 197–211), §L.29 (lines 2451–2538), the *Successor* lines of §L.30 to §L.39 (met by
a search for `^\*\*Successor`, read at each line with the lines after it) and Task 4's *Not written* list (lines
5404–5426); the carry list's §2, §4 and §5 (lines 38–149). The reading file was not opened; the site places no row.
(lxx) **§L.29 (1): the successor gains no statement for the catalog matches from this act; nothing is added to the
dispatch for it, and it is not listed in the report on its own. A derivation, not a question.**
- **What the point is.** §L.29's *Open* point (1): *"Ruling 1 gives L3 a new published fact (the matches), and the
  record orders the framework's amendment only. Whether the successor also gains a statement for it — as the key-areas
  record ordered for key areas (§L.13) — is not stated by this record"* (dispatch lines 2511–2514); its check note:
  *"Point (1) stands; §L.34 meets it again."* (line 2521).
- **What the record orders.** Ruling 1: *"The ratified framework is amended at three places (the writing is the
  application act's)"* (record line 37), the *Consumes* line, the *Publishes* line and §3.2's last sentence; it orders
  no text for the derivation or the successor (§L.29's *Successor* line, dispatch line 2463, checked at the record read
  whole). What is to be specified about the matches it leaves, in terms, to *"the amendment and L3's detail design"*,
  *"not decided here"* (record lines 53–60). The surface's position B, which Ruling 1 ratifies by name, places that specification later:
  *"A detail specification of the matching follows in L3's detail-specification work."* (surface lines 150–151).
- **Where a record wanted a statement, it said so.** The key-areas and modal-counts records: *"the derivation to gain a
  statement for them"* (the carry list's §4 item 5, lines 113–114); the bass-role record: *"the L3 charter's Publishes
  list … and the amended L3 specification gain the item, a new L3 statement being written there"* (§2 item 8, lines
  61–63; §5, line 126). Each orders both the charter amendment and the statement. The catalog-key record stands in the
  carry list's §2 (item 6, lines 55–58), for the charter only, and in neither §4 item 5 nor §5.
- **The act may not write one itself.** The standing bars: *"no derivation of any specification statement — an
  amendment is the record's words placed, never this batch's"* (dispatch line 201). A statement for the matches would
  need words no record gives.
- **No later catalog record orders one.** The *Successor* lines of §L.30 to §L.38 each read *"no amendment"* or *"no
  amendment ordered"* (dispatch lines 2551, 2643, 2725, 2816, 2898, 2979, 3058, 3165, 3249); several name the matches as
  published *"once the application act amends `FRAMEWORK.md`"*, which the dispatch reads as §L.29's amendments. §L.39
  adds one statement, the chord-suggestion tool's (*"It may read L3's published matches and the same catalog L3
  reads"*, lines 3344–3346); it presupposes the matches and is not a statement for them. §L.34's own pointer, *"See
  *Open* point (4)"* (line 2898), is A14's next site and is not judged here.
- **Where the fact comes back to the user.** The three charter amendments give no new text, so each is a
  STOP-and-report under form (e), listed in the report under *charter and contract amendments left to the user* (Task
  4's *Not written* item 2, dispatch lines 5411–5413). So the new published fact is already put before the user there.
- **Not listed again.** By the test (lix) to (lxix) set, a point is listed where its answer is unratified or absent.
  Here the ratified texts answer it: the record orders the charter only and leaves the matching's specification to the
  amendment and the detail design, and position B places that specification in later detail-specification work. So
  pointing §L.29's *Open* point (1) here drops nothing.
- **Two records.** The site places no row, so form (b)'s two-record rule does not arise.
- **The weak point.** That *"L3's detail-specification work"* is a later act, and not this act's successor (the amended
  L3 specification), is this side's reading. If the user reads it as this act's successor, the successor would need a
  statement with words that no record gives, which the standing bars exclude; the point would then be a
  STOP-and-report, listed among *the points that are not this batch's*, as at (lxii). Either way this act writes no
  statement for the matches. Documentation only either way.
**Documentation only; no algorithm change is decided here.** Ruling 1 is itself an algorithm change, ruled on
2026-11-06, not decided here: *"L3 publishes, as a read-off fact, which entries of the catalog of named progressions fit
which stretches of L2's decided chords: per match, the entry's name and the stretch of decided chords it covers,
overlapping matches all published. How a match is shown to an end user, and whether a preset filters what is shown, is
presentation, decided later and separately."* (record lines 32–35). Ruling 2's method stands as the record states it:
L2 decides key and chord together; its progression term is a table of chord-to-chord pairs counted once from annotated
music; DP-O stays open, the catalog term among its named candidates, unmeasured and unadopted (record lines 72–75). Not
applied to any row by this sitting. **Not touched here:** §L.29's *Open* points (2) (settled by Task 4's *Not written*
item 2, as its check note says) and (3) (the DP-O note, A14's third site); §L.34 (4). **To do at release:** (Ruled: Ruling 28 of the part 2 ruling record, `records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_11_l3_application_release_part2_sitting.md` (its heading at that record's line 1462, its *To do at release* at lines 1522–1524), governs this point; the proposal after this note is kept as former wording (#12). form (e) and standing bars (lines 179–203), §L.29's check note at lines 2516–2523, §L.34 (lines 2882–2967), Task 4's
*Not written* list and the lines after it (lines 5404–5433), and searches of the whole dispatch for `^\*\*Successor`
