(lxxi) **§L.34 (4): the successor gains no statement for the substituted member and its operation from this act; the
charter half is already settled by Task 4's *Not written* item 2, which names §L.34; nothing is added to the dispatch,
and the point is not listed in the report on its own. A derivation, not a question.**
- **What the point is.** §L.34's *Open* point (4): *"The successor and the charter: the ruling adds to what L3 publishes
  (the substituted member and its operation in each match). As at §L.29's *Open* point (1), whether the successor gains
  a statement for it is not stated; and the charter's words are not given (above)."* (dispatch lines 2943–2945); its
  check note: *"Points (1) to (4) stand."* (line 2952). It has two halves, a successor half and a charter half.
- **What the record orders.** Ruling 1 states the method (record lines 28–32) and marks it *"**Algorithm: a change.**"*
  (line 36). Of where it is written it says only: *"Where the application act writes it is that act's (surface §1.3,
  marked as this side's reading: with key Ruling 1's amendment to the L3 *Publishes* line and §3.2)."* (lines 40–41).
  Its §3: it *"edits no tracked document (the framework's amendment is the application act's)"* (line 77). It orders no
  text for the derivation or the successor, as §L.34's *Successor* line says (dispatch line 2898), checked here at the
  record read whole. The surface says the same in its own words: *"If B is ratified, the application act states it with
  key Ruling 1's amendment to the L3 *Publishes* line and §3.2 **[my reading of where it is written]**"* (surface lines
  135–137). Neither names the successor as where the method is written: the record does not name it at all (a search
  of it for *"successor"* and *"specification"* meets nothing), and the surface names *"the amended L3 specification"*
  once, at line 116, only as where the catalog's final content is *"confirmed at the ratification act"*.
- **The successor half: no statement, on (lxx)'s grounds, tested here.** (1) The act may not write one with words no
  record gives: *"no derivation of any specification statement — an amendment is the record's words placed, never this
  batch's"* (dispatch lines 201–202). (2) Where a record wanted a statement it said so **[relayed from (lxx), which
  read the carry list's §4 item 5 and §5 at their lines]**; the carry list names
  substitution part 1 at its §2 item 6 only, for where the substitution matches are written (*"Substitution part 1 lines
  40–41: where the substitution matches are written "is that act's""*, carry list lines 57–58), and the search for
  *"substitut"* meets it nowhere else in the carry list, so it is in neither §4 item 5 nor §5, as (lxx) found for the
  catalog-key record. (3) No later record orders one: the *Successor* lines of §L.35 to §L.38 read *"no amendment"*
  (dispatch lines 2979, 3058, 3165, 3249), and §L.39's one statement is the chord-suggestion tool's (line 3344), as
  (lxx) records. (4) A further ground of this record's own: among what it names as *"Not decided … waiting"* is *"the
  carrier form"* (record line 52), which the surface explains as *"part 1 §3.2's (per match, or a non-tiling span
  kind)"* (surface line 128). How a match carries the substituted member is itself left open, so a statement written
  now would decide a point the ruling names as waiting. The detail specification of the matching is placed later by
  the key record's position B (*"A detail specification of the matching follows in L3's detail-specification work"*,
  **[relayed from (lxx); the key surface not read by this sitting]**).
- **The charter half: already settled by the dispatch.** Task 4's *Not written* item 2 reads: *"The catalog matches at
  three places — the L3 *Consumes* line, the L3 *Publishes* line, and §3.2's last sentence (§L.29, which says what is to
  change and gives no new text; §L.34 places the substitution matches' writing with them). Carry list §2 item 6."*
  (dispatch lines 5411–5413), under the heading *"Not written — listed in the report for the user, under *charter and
  contract amendments left to the user*"*, each item *"a STOP-and-report under form (e) because the record gives no
  words"* (lines 5404–5407). Form (e): *"where the record gives no words, that amendment is a STOP-and-report, the rest
  applied"* (lines 183–184). §L.34's own *Charter* paragraph reaches the same: *"Under form (e), STOP-and-report unless
  the Task supplies words as authored and overrulable"* (line 2906), and Task 4 supplies none. This is how §L.29's check
  note settled §L.29's point (2): *"Point (2) is settled by Task 4 *Not written* item 2: no words, STOP-and-report,
  listed for the user."* (lines 2520–2521). So *"the charter's words are not given"* is answered: the act writes no
  charter words for the substitution matches, and the item is listed for the user, the substituted member and its
  operation with it.
- **Where the fact comes back to the user.** Under item 2, among *charter and contract amendments left to the user*.
  So the new published fact (the substituted member and its operation in each match) is put before the user there, as
  (lxx) found for the matches themselves.
- **Not listed again.** By the test (lix) to (lxx) set, a point is listed where its answer is unratified or absent.
  Here the ratified texts answer it: the record places the writing with the charter amendments and orders no successor
  text, and leaves the carrier form waiting; the dispatch already lists the charter item for the user. So pointing
  §L.34's *Open* point (4) here drops nothing.
- **Two records.** The site places no row, so form (b)'s two-record rule does not arise.
- **The weak points.** (a) As at (lxx): that the later detail-specification work, and not this act's successor, is where
  the matching's specification goes is this side's reading. If the user reads it as this act's successor, the successor
  would need a statement with words no record gives, which the standing bars exclude; the point would then be a
  STOP-and-report, listed among *the points that are not this batch's*, as at (lxii). (b) The record itself marks the

**Documentation only; no algorithm change is decided here.** Ruling 1 is itself an algorithm change, ruled on

operation. L2's chord and Roman numeral are unchanged."* (record lines 28–32). The method that stands with it: key

chord-to-chord pairs, DP-O open with the catalog term an unmeasured candidate (record lines 38–40). Not applied to any row
by this sitting. **Not touched here:** §L.34's *Open* points (1) (A1 and A2), (2) (sorted at (xxiii)) and (3) (sorted at

(lxxii) **§L.29 (3), the DP-O note: its site is the L2 nothing-from-L3 record's note, which the L2 application act had
quoted beside L2-S31 in the L2 successor; the catalog-key record's Ruling 2 states the addition whole, and that record is

- **What the point is.** §L.29's *Open* point (3): *"The DP-O note: its site, its text and whether this act writes it
  are not stated by the record. Until (2) and (3) are settled, those amendments are STOP-and-report."* (dispatch lines
  2514–2516). Its check note: a search of the Tasks finds the note nowhere in them, *"So as the Tasks stand it reaches
  the report only as this entry's own STOP-and-report (Task 1 (c) item 6), not under Task 4's heading *charter and
  contract amendments left to the user*"* (lines 2522–2525). Ruling 2: the catalog term *"is added to the DP-O note as a
  named candidate, beside the longer chord-to-chord memory, the cadential-formula term, the sequence term and harmonic
  rhythm"*, *"Documentation only. Algorithm: no change."* (record lines 64–72).
- **The site.** The four candidates Ruling 2 names beside the catalog term are those of the L2 nothing-from-L3 record's
  section *"A note for whoever takes up DP-O — recorded on the user's instruction"* (its lines 88–106): *"a longer
  chord-to-chord memory, a cadential-formula term, or the sequence term"* (lines 97–98) and, in its paragraph *"Added by
  Ruling 4:"*, harmonic rhythm (lines 103–104). The catalog-key surface names that record's note as the one meant (*"Its
  recorded note for DP-O names …"*, surface lines 63–65; *"What the DP-O note does not say"*, line 76). `FRAMEWORK.md`'s
  DP-O (lines 894–904) names no candidate term: it asks whether to adopt a hierarchy, with a tree-against-sequence
  falsifier, as the note itself says (*"`FRAMEWORK.md` DP-O asks only whether to adopt a hierarchy"*, record line 95).
  The L2 application dispatch had the note quoted whole beside L2-S31 in the L2 successor, *"the record being its home
  either way"* (its item 4, lines 2276–2285) **[that it was so written is relayed from that dispatch; the L2 successor
  not opened]**.
- **The text, and where the addition is recorded.** A precedent for adding to the note is Ruling 4 of the same L2
  record, which wrote its addition into the note in that record (*"Harmonic rhythm is added to the DP-O note above"*,
  line 166; the note's *"Added by Ruling 4:"* paragraph). The catalog-key record states its addition whole in Ruling 2's
  bold sentence, with what it changes (*"Algorithm: no change"*) and what travels with it, and gives no further text. So
  the addition is recorded in the catalog-key record, as Ruling 4's is in its own; no new text is needed, and none is
  derived here.
- **Whether this act writes it: no.** Each place this sitting found for it is closed to the act. The L2 successor is
  barred in terms: *"It does not edit … `cowork_derived_specification_l2_2026_10_10.md` or IC-L2"* (dispatch lines
  5432–5433). `FRAMEWORK.md` is edited *"at Edits 1 to 3 and their notes only"* (line 5430), and its DP-O carries no
  candidate list for the term to join. A ruling record is not edited. The L3 successor would need text no record orders,
  which the standing bars exclude (*"an amendment is the record's words placed, never this batch's"*, line 201), and the
  candidate is an L2 term: DP-O's question, *"what chord follows what"*, is *"inside L2's charter"* (`FRAMEWORK.md` line
  902), and Ruling 2's term rates *"each competing reading"* in L2's choice of chords. So point (3) leaves no amendment
  STOP-and-report: none is ordered, and Task 1 (c) item 6 does not reach it. Nor does it belong under *charter and
  contract amendments left to the user*, for the note is neither a charter nor an interface contract.
- **Listed, because a copy is left that this act may not touch.** By the test (lix) to (lxxi) set, a point is listed
  where its answer is unratified or absent. Ruling 2 is ratified and its record holds it. But the L2 successor's
  quotation of the note, made from the L2 record's paragraphs as they stood, does not name the catalog term **[this
  sitting's reading of the L2 dispatch's item 4; the L2 successor not opened]**, and neither the catalog-key record nor
  this dispatch says whether that copy is brought up to date, or by which act. That is (lxi)'s case, a point at L2 this
  act may not settle. So it goes under *the points that are not this batch's* (Task 5, dispatch lines 5518–5521), named
  there at release with Ruling 2's sentence, so that pointing §L.29's *Open* point (3) here does not drop it.
- **Two records.** The site places no row, so form (b)'s two-record rule does not arise. The search of the whole
  dispatch meets *"DP-O note"* at §L.29 (lines 2484, 2486, 2515, 2523), the check row (line 20) and §L.47's quotation of
  an alternative its record did not take (line 4114); no other entry writes the note.
- **The weak point.** That the L2 record's section is *"the DP-O note"* Ruling 2 means, and not a new note in L3's own
  documents, is this side's reading, resting on the surface's naming of that note and on the four candidates Ruling 2
  lists beside the catalog term, which are that note's. If the user wants the addition written somewhere by this act,
  the home this sitting finds open to it is the L3 successor, with words he supplies at release; that would file
  documentation only. Documentation only either way.
**Documentation only; no algorithm change.** The method stands as Ruling 2 states it (record lines 72–75): L2 decides
key and chord together; its progression term is a table of chord-to-chord pairs counted once from annotated music, so a
progression longer than two chords is weighed link by link, not whole; DP-O stays open, its named candidates unmeasured
and unadopted; adopting a catalog term after a measurement would be its own ruling. Not applied to any row by this
sitting. **Not touched here:** §L.29's *Open* points (1) (sorted at (lxx)) and (2) (settled by Task 4's *Not written*
item 2, as its check note says); the L2 successor. **To do at release:** (Ruled: Ruling 24 of the part 2 ruling record, `records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_11_l3_application_release_part2_sitting.md` (its heading at that record's line 1228, its *To do at release* at lines 1283–1287), governs this point, *"its proposed Task 5 naming not used"* (the ruling's words); the proposal after this note is kept as former wording (#12). Pointer added at release, step 5 part 4, 2026-11-17, by the sitting booted on entry 1075, on the release check's finding 42; nothing replaced.) change §L.29's *Open* point (3) (dispatch lines

**§L.27 (2), part E** (2026-11-11, the sitting booted on entry 717). Read at the objects: the architecture-rules record
whole; its surface, `ratification_surfaces/cowork_l3_tier3_architecture_rules_surface_2026_11_04.md`, at its headings,
its §6 (lines 162–195) and its §8 to §12 (lines 227–311), and by a search for *"Part E"*, *"part E"*, *"38.47"* and
*"38.48"*; the dispatch's §L.27 (lines 2268–2374) and Task 1 (c) (lines 5029–5068); this list's A1 and A2 rule (iv) and
items (lxx) to (lxxii). The reading file was not opened; the site places no row.
(lxxiii) **§L.27's part E: the added statement's text is the record's quoted sentence, by Task 1 (c) item 3 (i), quoted
whole; (ii) does not apply to part E. A derivation, not a question.**
- **What the point is.** §L.27's check note: *"For part E, both item 3 (i) (the record quotes the sentence) and (ii) (the
  part stands as the surface states it) describe it, and item 3 does not say which governs when both do; for the release
  surface, not settled here."* (dispatch lines 2363–2365). Item 3: (i) *"where the record quotes the statement's text, or
  gives it as an "As ruled" sentence, that text, quoted whole"*; (ii) *"where the record says a part "stands as the
  surface states it after the check", that part as the surface file the record names states it, quoted whole, located by
  the part's heading there, with the record's "In short" words for the part quoted beneath it"* (lines 5050–5054). Both
  describe part E: the record quotes its sentence (record lines 48–50), and says of the parts *"Each part stands as the
  surface states it after the check"* (line 27).
- **The record's own words settle it.** Item 3 stands under (c)'s rule *"where a record's own words and this settlement
  differ, the record governs"* (dispatch line 5030). For part E the record does not leave the text to the surface: the
  ruling's title is *"parts A to F RATIFIED as derivations, part E in the narrower wording"* (line 25); part E is
  *"ADOPTED in the narrower wording"*, followed by that wording in quotation marks (lines 48–50); §0 says the question put
  was *"ratification as derivations, part E in a narrower wording"* (lines 19–20); the documentation sentence repeats
  *"in part E's wording"* (line 59). So the wording is what the user ratified for part E, given by the record as a
  sentence; (i) is that case.
- **(ii) would write the wording the ruling declines.** The surface's part E, located by its heading (§6, lines
  162–195), quotes Row 38.47's proposal, *"… and never by a backward edge."* (lines 165–166), which the record says *"is
  not adopted as written"* (line 50). Quoted whole as the statement's text, as (ii) orders, it would put that sentence
  inside the statement. Under (i) it is not written there.
- **The ruled wording is kept.** The surface's §6 derives *"that a reading missing from L3's own candidate
  readings be remedied inside L3, by widening its candidate set; a backward edge is not the remedy, except as the
  deliberate, surfaced, measured and documented exception D-466 admits."* (lines 185–187), and its check says *"part E's
  narrowing stands"* (line 292). The record's sentence differs from it only in its opening (*"A reading missing … is
  remedied"* in place of *"that a reading missing … be remedied"*), the change from a proposal to a statement. The
  surface's grounds and weak points stay in the surface, which *"stands alone and is not reproduced here (#6)"* (record
  line 16).
- **What the dispatch already writes.** §L.27's amendment 1 gives part E as *"the quoted sentence"* (dispatch lines
  2287–2289), so (i) needs no new words. Parts A to D and F are written under (ii), as the check note settles (lines
  2362–2363), and are not touched.
- **The rows.** Rows 38.47 and 38.48 take their lines under A1 and A2 rule (iv): no *as proposed*, the placement in the
  record's words. That is the rows' line, not the statement's text, and this item does not change it.
- **Two records.** The site places no row of its own, so form (b)'s two-record rule does not arise.
- **The weak point.** That (i) governs where both (i) and (ii) describe a part is this side's reading, from (c)'s rule and
  the record's words above; item 3 states no order between them. If the user wants (ii) for part E, the statement would
  carry the surface's §6 whole, the record's sentence beneath it, and the reading file's declined proposal sentence
  inside it as a quotation. Documentation only either way.
**Documentation only; no algorithm change.** The record: *"**Algorithm: no change.**"* (line 62). The method stands as the
record states it (its lines 62–67): L3 reads everything L2 publishes (its reading, its rivals with mass) plus L0's and
L1's published facts, and nothing else; never revises L2, and L2 reads nothing L3 publishes; a backward edge is tested by
measurement as soon as there is reason to suspect it helps; L3's cost scales with the working span and its publication
covers exactly the working span; a ruled-out reading is published, not dropped; evidence-class facts are published
broadly, each with its establishment status; which effort dials exist, and their defaults, is deferred (D-202). Not
applied to any row or statement by this sitting. **Not touched here:** §L.27's *Open* points (1) (A1 and A2) and (3)
(Row 8.105 (ii)); parts A to D and F. **To do at release:** (Ruled: Ruling 34 of the part 2 ruling record, `records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_11_l3_application_release_part2_sitting.md` (its heading at that record's line 1890, its *To do at release* at lines 1955–1958), governs this point; the proposal after this note is kept as former wording (#12). Pointer added at release, step 5 part 4, 2026-11-17, by the sitting booted on entry 1075, on the release check's finding 42; nothing replaced.) change the check note's sentence *"For part E, both item 3
(i) … not settled here."* (dispatch lines 2363–2365) to point here; in Task 1 (c) item 3, after (iii), add that for
§L.27's part E, which (i) and (ii) both describe, (i) governs, pointing here; former wordings kept.

**§L.10** (2026-11-11, the sitting booted on entry 718). Read at the objects: the research-registry record
(`records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_03_research_registry_sitting.md`) whole; the carry list whole, and a
search of it for *"phase-definition"*, *"phase definition"*, *"registry"* and *"research"*; the dispatch's §L.10 (lines
807–830), Task 5's close at lines 5506–5524 and *What this batch does NOT do* (lines 5544–5558), and a search of the
whole dispatch for *"phase-definition"*, *"research registry"* and *"§L.10"*; this list's items (lix) to (lxxii). The
phase-definition surface was not opened; the site places no row.
(lxxiv) **§L.10: this act does not carry the amendment of the phase-definition surface; nothing is added to the dispatch
for it; the record's sentence is listed in the report among *the points that are not this batch's*. A derivation, not a
question.**
- **What the point is.** §L.10's *Open* note: *"the record does not say which application act it means, and the
  phase-definition surface is not an L3 document. Whether this dispatch carries that amendment is to be checked at the
  carry list before release … If the carry list does not name it, this entry orders nothing and the sentence stays with
  the record."* (dispatch lines 823–826). The check row says the note stands, *"the carry list not read by this sitting
  either"* (line 20). The record's sentence, in its §2, *"What this ruling does NOT do"*: *"Whether the phase-definition
  surface's text is amended to name the step is left to the application act; this record and the 2026-10-07 record are
  the step's home until then."* (record lines 89–90).
- **The carry list does not name it.** Read whole, it has no item for the phase-definition surface or the research
  registry, and the search of it for the four terms above meets nothing. Its method accounts for this: it searched
  *"every L3 ruling record in `records/cowork/rulings/` dated 2026-11-02 to 2026-11-08 (the file names carrying `l3_`)"*
  (carry list lines 16–17), and the research-registry record's file name carries no `l3_`, as §L.10 says (dispatch line
  815). So the condition §L.10's note sets is met, and by the note's own words the entry orders nothing.
- **No other part of the dispatch carries it.** The search of the whole dispatch meets *"phase-definition"* at §L.10 only
  (lines 820 and 823). It meets *"research registry"* at §L.10 and in the construction table (line 14), and *"§L.10"*
  besides at the check row (line 20) and line 253; and line 2447 quotes another record citing *"research-registry Ruling
  1"*. None of these orders an edit to the phase-definition surface. The write set excludes it in terms: *"The landed
  paths of Task 0, the successor, the derivation's banner, the reading file's additions, the four `FRAMEWORK.md` sites,
  S-50, the Task-5 files and the report are the whole write set."* (lines 5556–5558). So this act does not write the
  amendment, whichever application act the record means.
- **The step keeps its home.** The record names the step's home until any amendment: *"this record and the 2026-10-07
  record"*. Nothing here changes that.
- **Listed, because the record leaves it open and this act may not settle it.** By the test (lix) to (lxxii) set, a
  point is listed where its answer is unratified or absent. Ruling 1 is ratified, but whether the phase-definition
  surface is amended the record leaves open, handing it to an act it does not name, and this act may not write it. That
  is (lxi)'s and (lxxii)'s case, a point outside this act's write set. It is not a point *answered by a record's own
  derivation*, for the record derives no answer; nor an *amendment with no words from the record*, for the record orders
  no amendment, it leaves open whether one is made; nor a *charter and contract amendment left to the user*, for the
  surface is neither a charter nor an interface contract. So it goes under *the points that are not this batch's* (Task
  5, dispatch lines 5518–5521), named there at release with the record's sentence, so that pointing §L.10's *Open* note
  here does not drop it.
- **Two records.** The site places no row, so form (b)'s two-record rule does not arise.
- **The weak point.** That which application act the record means need not be settled before release is this side's
  reading, resting on the write set, which excludes the surface either way. If the user reads the sentence as meaning
  this act and wants the amendment made by it, the dispatch would need the surface added to its write set and words for
  the amendment, which the record does not give (it says only *"to name the step"*). That would file documentation only.
  Documentation only either way.
**Documentation only; no algorithm change.** The record: *"It files a plan only: it changes no specification and no
algorithm as any specification defines it."* (record lines 4–5). The method stands as the record states it (its lines
86–88): *"the phase order detail specifications → research iteration (2026-10-07) → measurement design → audit → fix
plan; L3's method is `FRAMEWORK.md` §5's L3 block with DP-I"*. Not applied to any row by this sitting. **Not touched
here:** what the record carries as *"Still UNSETTLED"* (the snapshot's form; the 2026-08-30 bar on the workbook), of
which §L.10 says the batch settles nothing (dispatch lines 828–830); the other A16 sites. **To do at release:** (Ruled: Ruling 25 of the part 2 ruling record, `records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_11_l3_application_release_part2_sitting.md` (its heading at that record's line 1289, its *To do at release* at lines 1340–1343), governs this point, *"its proposed Task 5 naming not used"* (the ruling's words); the proposal after this note is kept as former wording (#12). Pointer added at release, step 5 part 4, 2026-11-17, by the sitting booted on entry 1075, on the release check's finding 42; nothing replaced.) change

- **Not listed.** By the test (lix) to (lxxiv) set, a point is listed where its answer is unratified or absent. Here

here: L1's published list (IC-L1 S-50) gains a restatement relation over the notes, its matching measure UNESTABLISHED

**§L.12 (5) and (6)** (2026-11-11, the sitting booted on entry 720). Read at the objects: the sections record whole; the
dispatch's §L.11 (lines 832–899), §L.12 (lines 901–1000) and Task 1 (c) item 1 (lines 5032–5040), and a search of the
whole dispatch for *"family (iv)"* and for the six rows named below; the reading file's §7 at its L3-S22 to L3-S24
entries (lines 78591–78604) and, at §6, Rows 19.39 and 57.352, each read by offset from its heading (lines 24490,
74531). Rows 36.50, 38.61, 38.72 and 40.5 were not read at §6. The reading file was searched only for row headings.
(lxxvi) **§L.12 (5) and (6): point (5) is settled by Task 1 (c) item 1, as §L.11's point (1) is; point (6), the check
sum, closes at the reading file's §7: the 30 rows the table places are 30 of the 36, the other six are AGREES rows that
no §L entry places, and they get no line. Nothing is added to the dispatch. A derivation, not a question.**
- **What the points are.** §L.12's *Open* note: *"(5) Amendment 3: see §L.11's *Open* point (1). (6) The check sum
  above. Until (1) to (4) and (6) are settled, those rows and amendments are STOP-and-report."* (dispatch lines
  973–974); its check note: *"Points (1), (3), (5) and (6) stand."* (line 980). The check sum (lines 951–954): *"the
  table places 12 + 4 + 1 + 10 + 3 = 30 row entries. The record also names Rows 57.352 and 19.39 (iii) as agreeing with
  Ruling 1's item (2), and places neither. **Whether these 32 are the 36 of §0, and which rows of the 36 the record
  leaves unnamed, is not established by this entry** (… whether the four section-side rows are among the 36 is not
  stated by the record)."*
- **Point (5): already settled in the dispatch.** Amendment 3 (L3-S23 family (iv)'s *"not filled"*) is, in §L.12's own
  words, *"the same amendment §L.11 amendment 4 orders … it is ONE amendment, and §L.11's handling binds it"* (lines
  921–923). §L.11's check note: *"Point (1) is settled by Task 1 (c) item 1: STOP-and-report, no words supplied."*
  (lines 887–888). Task 1 (c) item 1 names both entries, *"L3-S23 family (iv)'s "not filled" (§L.11 amendment 4 and
  §L.12 amendment 3, one amendment)"*, as a STOP-and-report listed under *amendments with no words from the record*
  (lines 5035–5040). So point (5) stood in §L.12's check note only as a pointer to §L.11's point (1), which the same
  check settled. Neither record gives replacement words (the sections record: *"L3-S23's family (iv) to be updated from
  "not filled" to reading L1's relation"*, its line 67), and none are derived here.
- **Point (6): the 36, read at the reading file's §7.** The record's §0 gives its population as §7's lines
  78591–78604, *"27 DIFFERS — 26 at L3-S22, 1 at L3-S23, 0 at L3-S24 — and 9 AGREES"*. At those lines: L3-S22, AGREES 4
  (Rows 19.39 (iii), 38.61, 38.72, 57.352) and DIFFERS 26; L3-S23, AGREES 2 (Rows 36.50, 40.5 (iii)) and DIFFERS 1
  (Row 7.34); L3-S24, AGREES 3 (Rows 8.23, 8.35 (i), 8.113 (ii)) and DIFFERS 0. 4 + 26 + 2 + 1 + 3 = 36.
- **The 30 placed are 30 of the 36.** L3-S22's 26 DIFFERS (line 78594) are the table's twelve overtaken rows, its four
  section-side rows and Ruling 2's ten, 12 + 4 + 10 = 26, each found in that line's list and none of the list left
  over. Row 7.34 is L3-S23's one DIFFERS; Rows 8.23, 8.35 (i) and 8.113 (ii) are L3-S24's three AGREES. So the four
  section-side rows are among the 36, at L3-S22, and the table's 30 are the 27 DIFFERS and three of the AGREES.
- **The six not placed.** The other six of the 36 are AGREES rows: Rows 57.352 and 19.39 (iii), named by the record in
  Ruling 1's item (2) (*"rows 57.352 and 19.39(iii) agree"*, its lines 53–54) and in its check correction (4), on
  quotations joined across them (line 86); and Rows 38.61, 38.72, 36.50 and 40.5
  (iii), named by the record only in its §7 list of rows read (its lines 214–215). None of the six is in the three
  rulings' documentation paragraphs, which list the rows to be re-marked (its lines 62–69, 111–113, 155–159). The
  search of the whole dispatch meets Rows 38.61, 38.72, 36.50 and 40.5 nowhere, and Rows 57.352 and 19.39 (iii) only at
  §L.12 (lines 951–952 and 956) and in the check row (line 20), which records the check sum as held and places nothing.
  So no §L entry places any of the six, and the route ruling's *"Rows no family reaches stay proposals"* governs, which
  the dispatch reads *"So a row no record names gets NO line"* (line 101) **[relayed from (xl) and (lvi); line 101 not
  read by this sitting]**. §L.12's own *"Rows the record mentions and does not place"* already gives *"No line from this
  record"* for Rows 57.352 and 19.39 (iii) and for *"the rows its §7 lists as read"* (lines 956–958). Each keeps its
  proposal.
- **The two named rows' proposals agree with the ruling.** At §6 Row 19.39 (iii) is proposed **ADOPTED — carried**
  (L3-S22) (line 24509), and Row 57.352 **ADOPTED — carried** (L3-S22), *"as Row 19.39 (iii)"* (line 74554); each
  AGREES with L3-S22's *"A section boundary is always a boundary of the phrase reading"*, the content of Ruling 1's item
  (2). So the proposal that stands is the word a line would carry.
- **So point (6) closes, and its STOP-and-report falls on nothing.** Which rows of the 36 the record leaves unplaced is
  established: the six above. The 30 rows take their lines as this list sorts them ((x), (xi), (liii), (liv), and the
  table's form (b-H) line for Ruling 2's ten), and the six take none.
- **Two records.** Row 19.39's other claims are other entries' (§L.12's own list, lines 960–962; (l)); claim (iii) gets
  no line, so form (b)'s two-record rule does not arise for it, nor for the other five.
- **The weak point.** That the record's *"rows 57.352 and 19.39(iii) agree"* names the two rows as support for item (2)
  and does not place them is this side's reading, resting on the documentation paragraphs, which list the rows to be
  re-marked and not these two. If the user reads it as placing them, each line would be **ADOPTED — carried (L3-S22)**,
  as proposed, the word each row already proposes; only whether a ruled line is written would change. Rows 36.50,
  38.61, 38.72 and 40.5 (iii) were not read at §6, and their proposals are not stated here; they get no line whatever
  they propose. Documentation only either way.
**Documentation only; no algorithm change.** Rulings 1 to 3 are themselves algorithm changes, ruled on 2026-11-03, not
decided here: sections are a weighed L3 decision over groups of whole phrases, on L3-S23's four families of evidence, no
single item forcing or forbidding a section boundary, published with rivals and their mass (Ruling 1); sections may
contain sections to any depth, each level tiling the level above (Ruling 2); each section gets a form-function name where
the chosen naming scheme has one, with rival names and their mass, UNESTABLISHED until measured, the naming scheme left
open (Ruling 3). Not applied to any row by this sitting. **Not touched here:** §L.12's *Open* points (1) to (3) (sorted
at (liii), (x) and (xi), and (liv)) and (4) (settled by its check note); the other A16 sites. **To do at release:** (Ruled: Ruling 35 of the part 2 ruling record, `records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_11_l3_application_release_part2_sitting.md` (its heading at that record's line 1960, its *To do at release* at lines 2055–2059), governs this point; the proposal after this note is kept as former wording (#12). Pointer added at release, step 5 part 4, 2026-11-17, by the sitting booted on entry 1075, on the release check's finding 42; nothing replaced.)

36 of §0, … is not established by this entry (…)"* (lines 952–954) with the accounting above, pointing here; in *"Rows
the record mentions and does not place"* (lines 956–958), name Rows 36.50, 38.61, 38.72 and 40.5 (iii) as the rows of

**§L.15 (1)** (2026-11-11, the sitting booted on entry 721). Read at the objects: the edge-of-span record whole; the
dispatch's form (b) and (b-H) (lines 155–167), §L.15 (lines 1156–1235), Task 3 (c) (lines 5172–5223) and *What this
batch does NOT do* (lines 5544–5558), and a search of the whole dispatch for *"7.67"* and, inside the check row (line
20), for *"§L.15"*; the reading file's §5 (lines 379–401), Row 7.67 at §6, read by offset from its heading (lines
4215–4229), and §10 from its L2 heading (line 79125) to line 79184, by offset; this list's A1 and A2 rules and items
(xiv), (xv), (lvi) and (lvii). The derivation was not opened; L3-S40 is quoted as the reading file quotes it.
(lxxvii) **Row 7.67: ADOPTED — carried (L3-S40), form (b), the line quoting the record's sentence; not *as proposed*.
The record orders the line. The row's §10 entry gets one added line saying the row stays at L3, every text kept; that
line's wording is the writing side's and goes on the release surface with Part B. A derivation, not a question.**
- **What the point is.** §L.15's *Open* point (1): *"Row 7.67: the record writes "may be re-marked ADOPTED — carried
  (L3-S40)", in the paragraph its ruling files as documentation, and says the re-marking is this act's. Whether "may be"
  orders the line or only allows it is to be checked at the record before release; on the writing side's reading it
  orders it, as part of Ruling 1's documentation. If the line is written, the row comes off the transfer list at §10 if
  it is there"* (dispatch lines 1209–1213); its check note: *"Points (1) and (2) stand."* (lines 1225–1226). The
  record's §2, under *"Documentation:"*: *"Row 7.67 (proposed RELOCATED to L2 only because L3's right to ask was open)
  may be re-marked ADOPTED — carried (L3-S40). The re-marking is the application dispatch's act."* (record lines 78–80).
- **What §6 proposes.** The row is one claim (outgoing: *"A decision whose span is cut by the **selection edge** before
  any of (i)–(iii) holds fires this layer's **extension request** per the one cross-layer extension spec"*). §6 names
  L3-S40 as speaking to it, **AGREES** (*"L3-S40 has a fact whose extent crosses the edge ask "for a larger span from the
  caller, as L2 does""*), and proposes **RELOCATED** — to ***L2 — The tonal reading. The one entangled decision.***, on
  the ground *"the decisions this span serves are L2's, and the derivation's L3-S40 cites L2-S22 for L2 being "the layer
  that asks for more music"; whether L3 may itself request is OQ-L3-10, listed at §8 and put to nobody."* (lines
  4217–4229).
- **The record orders the line.** (1) Its next sentence gives the re-marking to this act in terms: *"The re-marking is
  the application dispatch's act."* (2) The sentence stands in the same *Documentation* paragraph as *"Recorded as
  decided against: … Rows 8.64, 8.65, 54.14(ii), 54.36(i), and the tag-only part of Rows 54.14(i) and 54.36(ii)"*, which
  the §L.15 table writes as form (b-H) lines (dispatch lines 1195–1196); the record says A was put *"stating that it
  changes the algorithm and also files documentation"* (its lines 22–23). (3) The parenthesis gives the RELOCATED proposal's
  only ground, *"only because L3's right to ask was open"*, and the row's own §6 proposal rests on the same question
  (*"whether L3 may itself request is OQ-L3-10"*). Ruling 1 answers it (*"L3 may ask the caller for more music"*, record
  line 50) and closes it (*"OQ-L3-10 closed"*, line 76). So *"may"* says the bar on the re-marking has gone, not that
  the act chooses: the act takes no ruling and weighs no position (*"No ruling taken, no position weighed"*, dispatch
  lines 5546–5550), so a permission with no rule for using it would leave the act nothing it could write.
- **The word: ADOPTED — carried (L3-S40), by A1 and A2 rule (ii)**, the record naming the L3-S number as carrying the
  content. §5 defines ADOPTED — carried as content *"a derived statement already carries … in substance (named by its
  L3-S-number)"* (line 382). The row AGREES with L3-S40 at §6, and the ruling confirms L3-S40 as written (A was put as
  *"L3-S40 as written"*, record lines 19–20; §L.15's amendment 3, *"Stands as derived."*, dispatch lines 1177–1178).
  Each other word fails at its own definition: not RELOCATED, for Ruling 1 gives L3 the asking, so the content no longer
  *"belongs to another charter"* (line 387); not ADOPTED — proposed, for a derived statement carries it; not
  QUARANTINED, for §6 reads the sentence as a rule of the design and does not say it describes the code; not DISCARDED,
  for no worth-test finding is given; not HISTORICAL, for the record re-marks the row ADOPTED and calls nothing in it
  superseded. A1 and A2 rule (v)'s STOP for a row proposed anything but an ADOPTED word does not fall on it: rule (v)
  goes with rule (i), whose word is read from the row's proposal; under rule (ii) the word is the record's, as (xiv) and
  (xv) applied rule (ii) to rows proposed UNPLACED **[this sitting's reading of rule (v)'s reach]**.
- **The line.** `*RULED DISPOSITION (cowork_rulings_2026_11_03_l3_edge_of_span_sitting.md, Ruling 1).* **ADOPTED —
  carried (L3-S40)** — "Row 7.67 (proposed RELOCATED to L2 only because L3's right to ask was open) may be re-marked
  ADOPTED — carried (L3-S40). The re-marking is the application dispatch's act."` The row is proposed RELOCATED, so the
  line does not say *as proposed*. It is one claim, so no part is named. This is (lvii)'s case, a row a ratified text
  names by number with what the act does, not (lvi)'s, rows no ruling names.
- **Two records.** The search of the dispatch meets *"7.67"* at §L.15 (lines 1190, 1197, 1199, 1209 and 1221) and in the
  check row (line 20), which records §L.15's check sum *"with Row 7.67"* and places nothing. So one record gives the row
  a line, and form (b)'s two-record rule is not met.
- **The transfer list.** §10 carries the row at the entry at line 79178, under the L2 heading (line 79125): *"Rows 7.60,
  7.61, 7.64, 7.65, 7.66, 7.67, 7.277 and 7.278 — the slice, the key-span and the decision-context span, with its extent
  and its extension request. (L3-S40 AGREES at Row 7.67, citing L2-S22.)"*. As the dispatch stands, nothing reaches the
  entry: Task 3 (c) item 5 appends RELOCATED placements only (lines 5204–5209), and item 6 adds its *LEAVES L3* line only
  beneath entries whose row is lined from §L.21 to §L.24 (lines 5210–5216). The entry would go on listing for transfer to
  L2 a row whose §6 line says L3 carries it, while §5 puts on §10 the rows that are RELOCATED (lines 387–388). §L.15's
  point says the row *"comes off the transfer list"*. Item 6's manner does that with every text kept (#12): ONE line added
  beneath the line-79178 entry, naming Row 7.67 only (item 6's *"the line names the rows it covers"*), the other seven
  rows untouched. Item 6's own form does not fit, for *LEAVES L3* would say the row leaves L3, and the ruling keeps it
  there. So the line takes a form of its own: `*STAYS AT L3 (cowork_rulings_2026_11_03_l3_edge_of_span_sitting.md,
  Ruling 1).* — Row 7.67, re-marked ADOPTED — carried (L3-S40): "proposed RELOCATED to L2 only because L3's right to ask
  was open".` Its wording is the writing side's, as item 6's is, overrulable under B7, and goes on the release surface
  with Part B.
- **The weak point.** That *"may be re-marked"*, read with *"The re-marking is the application dispatch's act"*, orders
  the line is this side's reading, as it was the dispatch writer's. If the user reads *"may"* as a permission only, the
  row keeps its proposed RELOCATED as a proposal, gets no line and stays on §10 as it stands, its proposal resting on a
  question Ruling 1 has answered; it is then listed among *the points that are not this batch's*, as at (lxii).
  Documentation only either way.
**Documentation only; no algorithm change.** Ruling 1 is itself an algorithm change, ruled on 2026-11-03, not decided
here: L3 may ask the caller for more music where its in-selection phrase or section reading depends on music beyond the
edge; it stops when its own in-selection phrases and sections stop changing between enlargements, under the safety cap
(D-624); it marks its publication truncated if the cap ends the loop (D-263); units cut by the edge carry their mark
(record lines 50–53). Not applied to any row by this sitting. **Not touched here:** §L.15's *Open* points (2) (sorted at
(xiv)), (3) (settled by Task 1 (c) item 6, as its check note says) and (4) (answered at amendment 2); the other seven rows
of the line-79178 §10 entry; the other A16 sites. **To do at release:** change §L.15's *Open* point (1) (dispatch lines
1209–1213), the check note's *"Points (1) and (2) stand."* (lines 1225–1226) for point (1), and the check row's
*"§L.15 points (1) and (2)"* (line 20) for point (1), to point here; write the word into the §L.15 table's line for Row
7.67 (dispatch line 1197); add to Task 3 (c) item 6 the §10 line above for Row 7.67, pointing here; former wordings kept.
**§L.23 (2)** (2026-11-11, the sitting booted on entry 722). Read at the objects: the L3-S38 ratification record whole;
its surface, `ratification_surfaces/cowork_l3_s38_ratification_surface_2026_11_03.md`, whole; the first-cut and
second-cut records whole; the dispatch's form (b) and (b-H) (lines 155–167), §L.21 to §L.23 (lines 1760–2004) and Task
3 (c) items 5 and 6 (lines 5204–5223), and a search of the whole dispatch and of the ratification record for the twenty
row numbers; this list's items (xli) to (xlvi) and (lv). The reading file was not opened; §6 and §10 were not read for
this site.
(lxxviii) **§L.23 (2), the 20 rows ruled earlier: each gets its earlier ruling's line only, from §L.21 or §L.22; no
second line naming the ratification record's Ruling 1. A derivation, not a question.**
- **What the point is.** §L.23's *Open* point (2): *"The 20 rows ruled earlier: on the writing side's reading each gets
  its earlier ruling's line only, because the record counts them as ruled already and ratifies "the other 80" with the
  statement. Whether they also get a line naming this Ruling 1 is to be confirmed before release."* (dispatch lines
  1977–1979). Its check note: Ruling 1's *"the 100 DIFFERS as the eight cuts file them"* *"reaches the 20 earlier rows by
  count, not by row, which does not settle whether they also get a line naming it"* (lines 1994–1996).
- **Which rows, checked at the records and at the table lines.** First-cut Ruling 1's twelve: Rows 2.3 (i), 7.15, 7.28
  (ii), 7.55, 7.130, 7.131, 7.132, 7.149, 7.150, 7.191, 7.194, 7.311 (first-cut record lines 25–29; §L.21's table,
  dispatch line 1779). Second-cut Ruling 1's eight: Rows 10.23 (i), 10.147 (ii) and 10.178, and the first cut's 7.13 (i),
  7.17 (i), 7.46, 7.56 and 7.135, re-filed from QUARANTINED (second-cut record lines 31–37; §L.22's table, lines
  1849–1850). 12 + 3 + 5 = 20, as the ratification surface's table gives them (*"12 by first-cut Ruling 1 ("a"); 5 more
  by second-cut Ruling 1"*, *"3 by second-cut Ruling 1 ("A")"*, its lines 40–41; 20 at line 48). Each is placed
  HISTORICAL in those rulings' words, *"HISTORICAL — rules of a superseded design"*, from the comparison's proposal
  RELOCATED to L2, and §L.21's and §L.22's tables give each a form (b) line naming its ruling.
- **The ratification record sets the 20 apart from what it ratifies with the statement.** Its words: *"the 100 DIFFERS
  as the eight cuts file them — … 20 of them by the user's earlier rulings (first-cut Ruling 1, second-cut Ruling 1),
  the other 80 this side's derivation in their grounds, ratified with the statement as boundaries Ruling 2 did for its
  rows"* (record lines 33–36). The cuts file the 20 by those rulings: the first-cut record's §1b as amended (*"the twelve
  of Ruling 1, the five of that ruling"*, its lines 55–59) and the second-cut record's §2 (*"the three of Ruling 1"*;
  *"the five of this Ruling 1"*, its lines 66 and 75). So *"as the eight cuts file them"* gives the 20 the earlier
  rulings' placements, and the record names those rulings as their source. What ratifying adds is the grounds of the
  80: the strongest point against, as put, is that *"ratifying with the placements ratifies the 80 derived groundings
  too"* (record lines 45–47; the surface's lines 98–100). The record names none of the twenty row numbers (searched).
- **Why this differs from the 45 of cuts 3 to 8.** (xli) to (xlvi) gave each of the 45 a line naming Ruling 1, though
  Ruling 1 reaches them by count too. They are among *"the other 80"*, which the record grounds in this side's derivation
  and ratifies with the statement. The 20 are not: the record names other rulings for them, and each of those rulings'
  records orders the re-marking itself (*"The rows are re-marked … by the application dispatch, not now"*, first-cut
  record lines 69–70; second-cut record lines 89–90).
- **Why this differs from (lv).** (lv) wrote both records' lines because the later record placed the rows by number and
  said their re-marking was the act's. Here the later record does not name the 20 by number and counts them as placed by
  the earlier rulings.
- **The transfer list.** Item 6 adds its *LEAVES L3* line beneath a §10 entry whose row carries a ruled line from §L.21
  to §L.24 (dispatch lines 5212–5214). The 20 carry §L.21's or §L.22's line, so item 6 reaches them with those records'
  sentences, each of which orders the taking-off itself (*"the 33 rows other than 2.6 taken off the reading file's §10
  row by row"*, first-cut record lines 69–70; *"taken off the reading file's §10 row by row"*, second-cut record lines
  89–90). So writing no line from the ratification record drops nothing there. Whether and where the 20 stand on §10 was
  not read for this site.
- **Two records.** The search of the dispatch meets the twenty row numbers at §L.21 (lines 1765, 1779, 1783, 1809) and
  §L.22 (lines 1835, 1849, 1850, 1877), and otherwise only at §L.19's mention of L2 Row 7.13 (ii) (lines 1607, 1616), a
  row of the L2 reading file and another claim. The five re-filed rows are named at both entries; the first-cut record's
  amendment hands them to the second-cut record, so they take §L.22's line only, as §L.21's table says (line 1783). So
  each of the 20 carries one line, and form (b)'s two-record rule does not arise.
- **The weak point.** That *"as the eight cuts file them"* gives the 20 the earlier rulings' lines, and not also a line
  of its own, is this side's reading of the record's *"20 of them by the user's earlier rulings"*. If the user reads
  Ruling 1 as placing all 100 itself, each of the 20 gets a second form (b) line naming it, **HISTORICAL**, the same word
  on the same claim; under form (b) both are written and the row is listed under *rows named by more than one record*,
  and nothing stops. Documentation only either way.
**Documentation only; no algorithm change.** The record: *"**No change against the currently decided method**"* (its
lines 49–53): L3 reads everything L2 publishes (its reading, its rivals with mass) plus L0's and L1's published facts,
and nothing else; never revises L2; L2 reads nothing L3 publishes; a backward edge is tested by measurement as soon as
there is reason to suspect it helps; key areas are read off L2's principal reading, a new one at every change of
tonality. Not applied to any row by this sitting. **Not touched here:** §L.23's *Open* points (1) (sorted at (xli) to
(xlvi)), (3) and (4) (settled by its check note); §L.21's and §L.22's lines; the other A16 sites. **To do at release:** (Ruled: Ruling 36 of the part 2 ruling record, `records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_11_l3_application_release_part2_sitting.md` (its heading at that record's line 2061, its *To do at release* at lines 2126–2129), governs this point; the proposal after this note is kept as former wording (#12). Pointer added at release, step 5 part 4, 2026-11-17, by the sitting booted on entry 1075, on the release check's finding 42; nothing replaced.)
change §L.23's *Open* point (2) (dispatch lines 1977–1979), and the check note's *"Points (1) and (2) stand for the
releasing sitting"* (line 1994) for point (2), to point here; former wordings kept.
**§L.30 (2)** (2026-11-11, the sitting booted on entry 722, its second site). Read at the objects: the lead-rows record

(lxxx) **§L.40 (4), Row 40.61 on D-522: the *⚠gap* mark says where D-522 is written, not whether it holds; the
record's ADOPTED placement on D-522 stands, and nothing is added to the dispatch for the mark. A derivation, not a

- **What the point is.** §L.40's *Open* point (4): *"Row 40.61 rests on D-522, whose home is marked *⚠gap* at the
  index; the record did not open it."* (dispatch lines 3497–3498); its check note: *"Points (1) to (4) stand."* (line

  opened)"* (record lines 32–33) and carries *"What D-522's *⚠gap* mark at the index means (not opened)"* (line 57).
  The surface gives the same as the placement's weak point (its line 101).
- **What the mark is, read at the index.** The index is `DECISIONS.md`. D-522's row there reads *"Explaining an
  inference to the end user is a late-bound DISPLAY consumer of facts that already exist — not a new analysis | LIVE |
  — | `cowork_evidence_inventory.md` §8b ⚠gap"* (line 302), under the columns *ID | Decision | Status | Entry ratified
  | Home* (line 250). So the mark stands in the home column. The register's own key to that column: where the home is
  neither the layer's section of `ARCHITECTURE.md` nor the specification that owns the decision, *"the entry says which
  of five cases it is: a **documentation gap** (a decision that governs a layer or a component, not findable from that
  layer's section — it carries an `OPEN_ITEMS.md` row)"* (lines 55–59). D-522's full entry says it in those terms:
  *"⚠ **home is not the specification that owns it** — a documentation gap; see `OPEN_ITEMS.md`."* (`decisions/group_C.md`
  line 1019), its home section being *"§8b — `## 8b. Declared future consumers, named by the user (2026-07-13)`"*, a
  section that *"RECORDS FINDINGS"* (line 1021). The register counts 19 such entries (line 151).
- **The mark leaves the decision standing.** D-522's full entry: *"**Status.** LIVE · decided 2026-07-13 · ratified by
  user"* (line 1017), with its *Why* recorded (line 1015). The *"—"* in the index's *Entry ratified* column is about
  the register entry, which the key keeps apart from the decision (*"This is a different event from the decision
  itself"*, lines 47–48). So the mark does not say D-522 is unsupported, unratified or not in force; it says D-522 is
  written in a findings section of the evidence inventory and not in a specification.
- **So the record's method holds for the row.** The record adopts Row 40.61 by the architecture-rules ruling's method,
  rows lying in the home of a LIVE register entry adopted on that entry (the surface's R4, lines 82–86; its §2A, line
  101). At §6 the row's sentence lies in D-522's home, lines 215 to 224 of the evidence inventory (*"the whole of range
  9, on which this row's sentence lies"*, reading file lines 55098–55102), and D-522 is LIVE. The mark changes neither
  fact. The record's weak point is answered at the object, and the placement stands as ratified.
- **Nothing for the act to write from the mark.** The register's home classes are the register's; the record allocates
  no register identity and edits no tracked document (its §3), and §L.40's *Register* note says *"none"* (dispatch
  lines 3509–3510). Whether placing the row bears on D-522's home class is not this site's and is not judged here. A
  search of `OPEN_ITEMS.md` for *"D-522"* meets nothing; the row the key speaks of was not looked for further (the
  archive and the detail files were not searched).
- **A fact found at the row, for A1, not settled here.** At §6 Row 40.61 is proposed **UNPLACED**, with *"Derived
  statements that speak to it. None."* (reading file lines 55090–55096). A1 and A2 rule (ii) needs an L3-S number the
  record names, and the record names none; rule (i) reads the word from the row's proposal; and rule (v), whose
  parenthesis names §L.40's rows to be checked, makes a row proposed anything but an ADOPTED word a STOP-and-report. So,
  as A1's rules stand, Row 40.61's ADOPTED line meets rule (v). That is §L.40's *Open* point (1), under A1, and goes to
  the release surface with A1's rules; this item does not touch it.
- **Two records.** The search of the dispatch meets *"40.61"* at §L.40 only (lines 3460, 3473, 3493, 3497, 3505), and
  *"D-522"* there and at §L.27's *Register* note (line 2368), which names it as a ground and places no row. So form
  (b)'s two-record rule does not arise.
- **The weak point.** That the record's *"(not opened)"* asked only what the mark means, which the register's key

  documentation gap until that gap is closed), the row stays a STOP-and-report and the question is his. The record's

(lxxxi) **§L.44 (2), Row 32.1: one form (b) line, modelled on the twin's ruled line, giving the clause RELOCATED to *the
uncertainty surface* and the rest HISTORICAL, each part named in the record's words, with the record's recorded items
written beside them in the same line; not *as proposed*. The line names its *"Row 1.19"* as the L2 row it is. The

  for the releasing sitting"*; *"The L2 twin's ruled line was not read by that sitting."* (dispatch lines 3856–3861).

  match what the record says of it"* (dispatch lines 222–224); this content matches, so it does not stop the row. As at
  (xlix) and (lviii), the parts are the record's words, as form (b-H) names a part.
- **What the record says.** Ruling 1, 2C (the user's word *"yes"*): *"as its twin L2 Row 34.1 is ruled (1): Row 32.1,
  the product's core scope (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §16). The "musical language detector and graceful degradation" clause

  `ARCHITECTURE.md` §16."* (record lines 49–53). The record names two disposition words, one per part; it names the
  homes and the answering rulings only by reference to L2.

  (cowork_rulings_2026_10_10_l2_tail_scope_and_single_rows_sitting.md, Ruling 2). **RELOCATED to *the uncertainty

  disposition word were written as quoted text within the row's one ruled line. The surface's §1 quotes the L2 record
  in the same words (its lines 70–87).
- **So the items with no word are written as text in the line, as the twin's are.** The record orders them *"recorded
  beside the row"* and the homes *"as L2 records them"*; L2 records them in its ruled line, and the record places the row
  *"as its twin L2 Row 34.1 is ruled"*. (xxxvii) took the twin's ruled line as the model for 2A of the same record; the
  same holds here. Writing them nowhere would drop what the record orders recorded. They carry no disposition word, so

- **"Row 1.19" is the L2 row.** The words *"travelling with Row 1.19 and D-498"* are the L2 ruling's, quoted in a

  **RELOCATED to *the uncertainty surface*** for its clause "musical language detector and graceful degradation",

stands as it is for them; the record's sentence is listed in the report for the user among *the points that are not this

- **What the point is.** §L.44's *Open* point (3): *"The record's §2 carries seven further row-parts of member 23 to
  "after family 6, judged against the plan" (carry list §8 item 9); this record writes nothing for them."* (dispatch

  23.44, inside §L.44 only (lines 3818–3886), as its check note found (lines 3838–3841). The ten L3 ruling records of
  2026-11-08 meet none of them; their one hit for *"family 6"* is the phrase-end evidence record's ground for Row 40.12
  (iv) (its line 34). No ruling record in the rulings folder's listing is dated after 2026-11-08. So the route ruling's

  at §6 **[relayed]**) were not looked for at §10. Task 3 (c) item 5 reaches only row-parts a §L entry places (dispatch
  lines 5205–5206), so nothing is appended for any of the seven whatever §10 holds.
- **Listed, because the record names the gap and leaves it open.** By the test (lix) to (lxxx) set, a point is listed
  where its answer is unratified or absent. The record states that the seven are proposed at L3 otherwise than their
  ruled L2 twins, and the surface's §4 names the rule they meet (*"Ruling 1's method says no preset or mixture reaches
  L2"*, for the five); the record decides nothing and leaves where they go to *"after family 6, judged against the
  plan"*. That is (lxii)'s, (lxv)'s and (lxvi)'s case. It is not a point *answered by a record's own derivation*, nor an
  *amendment with no words from the record*. So it goes under *the points that are not this batch's* (Task 5, dispatch
  lines 5518–5521), named there at release with the record's §2 sentence whole, Rows 23.1 and 23.44 with it, so that
  pointing §L.44's *Open* point (3) here does not drop it.
- **Two records.** No §L entry gives any of the seven a line, so form (b)'s two-record rule does not arise.
- **The weak point.** That *"Whether they go to the application act as noted or onto a later surface"* leaves the
  choice open, and does not send the rows to this act to be re-marked, is this side's reading, resting on the route
  ruling as at (lxv). If the user reads it as sending them here, the act still has no word for them: their proposals
  (RELOCATED to L2 for five, against Ruling 1's method as the surface reads it; to *the uncertainty surface* for two),
  or 2A's RELOCATED outside the framework, which no ruling gives them; the surface's §4 marks its own lean as untested.
  The word would be a question for him. Documentation only either way.
**Documentation only; no algorithm change.** The record: *"**Documentation only. Algorithm: no change.**"* The method
stands as the record states it (its lines 60–65): L2 decides key and chord together and publishes the chord as a degree
in its tonality, and the tonality as tonic and mode; L3 publishes facts read off it (chord symbol, figured bass, harmonic
rhythm, cadence with type at each phrase end, phrase and section grouping, key areas, the pivot re-expression, the modal
counts per key area, and, once the application act amends `FRAMEWORK.md`, the catalog matches); no preset, style label
or idiom mixture reaches L2; no preset chooses the catalog L3 reads, and L3's method uses no idiom tag; a display turns
published facts into text; L3 owns the answers to its own questions. Not applied to any row by this sitting. **Not
touched here:** §L.44's *Open* point (1) (sorted at (xxxvii)); 2A's and 2B's lines; the L3 reading file's Row 1.19; the
other A16 site, §L.46 (3). **To do at release:** (Ruled, for point (3): Ruling 20 of the part 2 ruling record, `records/cowork/rulings/cowork_rulings_2026_11_11_l3_application_release_part2_sitting.md` (its heading at that record's line 984, its *To do at release* at lines 1042–1045), governs point (3); point (2) is Ruling 6's, which orders no edit to this list [relayed from the working file's *Working-list edits* section]; the proposal after this note is kept as former wording (#12). dispatch's §L.46 (lines 3970–4068), Task 3 (c) item 5 (lines 5204–5209), Task 4's *What this Task does not do* (lines
5428–5433), Task 5's close at lines 5516–5537 and *What this batch does NOT do* (lines 5544–5558), and a search of the
whole dispatch for *"40.12"*, *"lyric"* and *"§L.46"*; the carry list at a search for *"lyric"*, *"phrase-end-evidence"*,
*"family 8"* and *"40.12"*; the reading file's §5 (lines 379–401), Row 40.12 at §6, read by offset from its heading
(lines 54224–54250), and §10 from its heading to its L1 heading (lines 78925–79008). `FRAMEWORK.md`, the L0/L1
derivation (S-1, S-43) and Ruling 50 were not opened; the L2 twin was not read at the L2 reading file.
(lxxxiii) **§L.46 (3), Row 40.12 (iii): the line is written as ruled, RELOCATED to *L0 — The notated record*, naming
claim (iii) and carrying the record's *"beside the row"* words, as the §L.46 table gives it; Task 3 (c) item 5 appends
the row-part under §10's L0 heading, which does not carry it; nothing is added to L0's list; the L0 question the record
carries is listed in the report for the user among *the points that are not this batch's*. A derivation, not a
question.**
- **What the point is.** §L.46's *Open* point (3): *"Row 40.12 (iii)'s RELOCATED to L0 rests on an L0 question the record
  carries: "whether the notated record carries lyrics. It belongs to L0's input contract. An addition to a charter's
  list is the user's"; the line is written as ruled, and nothing is added to L0's list."* (dispatch lines 4046–4048).
  Its check note says item 5 reaches the row, that whether §10 has a heading for *L0 — The notated record* was not read,
  and *"Points (1) to (3) stand"* (lines 4058–4061); the check row lists *"§L.46 points (1) to (3)"* as left (line 20).
  The record places the row *"**RELOCATED to *L0 — The notated record*** (whether L0 carries lyrics); beside the row: if
  it does, they enter L3 as a candidate term under L3-S9 (iv)'s held-out rule"* (record line 33), and carries, under
  *"Carried, not decided by this ruling"*: *"The L0 question of 3C: whether the notated record carries lyrics. It belongs
  to L0's input contract. An addition to a charter's list is the user's (S-43, **[relayed]**)."* (record lines 62–63).
- **What §6 proposes.** Row 40.12 has six claims; (iii) is *"lyrics and verse structure as chorale phrase structure,
  probably redundant with fermatas"*, *"THE DERIVATION IS SILENT"*, proposed **UNPLACED** — *"a source of phrase evidence
  that no derived statement names; read: L3-S9's four families, and `FRAMEWORK.md` §5's list of what L0 gives, which
  names no lyrics"* (lines 54231–54245). So the line does not say *as proposed*.
- **The line: as ruled, and already in the dispatch.** Ruling 1 gives the word and the record gives the words beside the
  row; the §L.46 table writes them as a form (b) line naming claim (iii) only (dispatch line 3998). The destination is on
  §5's list of charters (*"***L0 — The notated record*** (the input contract)"*, line 398), so A6's question does not
  arise, as at (xxxix). The words beside the row state a condition; the act neither meets nor tests it (as at (lxiii)):
  it does not decide whether L0 carries lyrics and adds no candidate term. The surface's §4 says the same: 3C *"adds
  nothing to L3 unless L0 later carries lyrics"* (its line 325).
- **Nothing is added to L0's list: confirmed at the objects.** The record orders no amendment; it carries the L0
  question and says an addition to a charter's list is the user's. The write set holds no L0 document but the derived
  L0/L1 specification at S-50 (dispatch lines 5431–5432, 5556–5558), and `FRAMEWORK.md` is edited *"at Edits 1 to 3 and
  their notes only"* (line 5430). The search of the dispatch for *"lyric"* meets §L.46 only (lines 3978, 3998, 4033,
  4047), and the carry list's search meets nothing for *"lyric"*, *"family 8"* or *"40.12"* (its line 193 names the
  record for Rows 36.41 and 36.118 only, and line 80 for the method sentence).
- **The transfer list.** The row-part is placed RELOCATED out of UNPLACED, so Task 3 (c) item 5 reaches it (dispatch
  lines 5204–5209). §10's heading *"To *L0 — The notated record* (the input contract)."* stands at line 78930, its
  entries running to line 79006, the L1 heading at line 79008. That block carries Row 40.12 (vi) (the entry at line
  78977) and not claim (iii): no entry from line 78932 to line 79006 names it. So item 5 appends claim (iii) under the
  L0 heading, in its own entry form, its record and ruling in the parenthesis; its STOP for a destination with no heading
  does not apply. That answers, for L0, what the check note left unread. The entry at line 78977 is not touched. The
  heading for claim (iv) is (xxxviii)'s and is not touched.
- **Listed in the report, among *the points that are not this batch's*.** By the test (lix) to (lxxxii) set, a point is
  listed where its answer is unratified or absent. The record carries the L0 question open, decides nothing on it and
  names it the user's. It is not a point *answered by a record's own derivation*, for the record derives no answer; nor
  an *amendment with no words from the record*, for none is ordered; nor a *charter and contract amendment left to the
  user*, for the record orders no amendment of L0's list and leaves open whether one is made **[this sitting's reading of
  that heading's reach]**. That is (lxxiv)'s case. So it goes under *the points that are not this batch's* (Task 5,
  dispatch lines 5518–5521), named there at release with the record's §2 sentence, so that pointing §L.46's *Open* point
  (3) here does not drop it.
- **Two records.** The search of the whole dispatch meets *"40.12"* inside §L.46 only (lines 3991–4058). Claim (iv) has
  §L.46's own line ((xxxviii)), on another claim, so form (b)'s two-record rule is not met, by its present wording or by
  (xlix)'s.
- **The weak points.** (a) If the user reads the L0 question as a charter amendment left to him, it is listed under
  *charter and contract amendments left to the user* instead; only the report's heading changes. (b) The surface's 3C
  weak points stand as stated and were not tested here: whether L0/L1 Ruling 50's *"every performance and phrasing
  mark"* already takes in lyrics, and S-43 not opened. If Ruling 50 already takes lyrics in, the L0 question may already
  be answered; this item does not judge that. Documentation only either way.
